# OrganicSEOOps Decision Log

This file records accepted decisions and their rationale. New decisions should be appended; previous entries should not be silently rewritten when the direction changes.

## D001: Product Name

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Use `OrganicSEOOps` as the working product name and `organicseoops` as the repository name.
- Rationale: The name reflects SEO and organic growth without limiting the product to article generation.

## D002: Canonical Plan

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Keep the complete plan in `docs/PROJECT_PLAN.md` and require future implementation to preserve or explicitly update it.
- Rationale: Conversation history is not a durable project specification.

## D003: Organization And Project Hierarchy

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Organizations own users, projects, usage, provider configuration, and future subscriptions. Each project represents one primary domain.
- Rationale: This supports one user with several sites, future teams, agencies, and subscription limits.

## D004: Initial Projects

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: The first managed projects will be `transfertakip.com` and `airporttransferhub.com`.
- Rationale: They provide concrete initial use cases without becoming hard-coded platform behavior.

## D005: Public Registration

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Support normal public registration, email verification, login, logout, password reset, organization creation, and invitations.
- Rationale: OrganicSEOOps is intended to become a normal multi-user product rather than a permanently private internal dashboard.

## D006: Superadmin Surface

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Use Django Admin for platform superadmins initially. Use Angular organization settings for normal organization owners.
- Rationale: Django Admin already provides secure operational CRUD and avoids building a second internal product prematurely.

## D007: Browser Authentication

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Prefer secure HttpOnly cookie authentication with CSRF protection. Do not keep authentication tokens in local storage.
- Rationale: This reduces browser token exposure and fits the controlled Angular SPA and Django API deployment.

## D008: Technology Stack

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Use latest stable compatible Angular, Django, and Django REST Framework releases at scaffold time, with PostgreSQL, Redis, Celery, S3, EC2, and Docker.
- Rationale: These match existing experience and the workload.

## D009: Monorepo

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Keep frontend, backend, deployment configuration, and project documentation in one repository with separate top-level directories.
- Rationale: Product changes commonly cross API, UI, jobs, and deployment; a monorepo keeps versions and guidance aligned.

## D010: Separate Service Containers

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: PostgreSQL, Redis, API, worker classes, Celery Beat, and reverse proxy run as separate containers. API and Celery containers may reuse the same backend image.
- Rationale: This preserves process isolation and independent scaling without duplicating images.

## D011: Initial Production Topology

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Start backend services on one EC2 instance using Docker Compose, with persistent EBS storage and S3 backups.
- Rationale: It is the simplest cost-conscious deployment, with an acknowledged single point of failure and documented managed-service migration path.

## D012: Frontend Hosting

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Build Angular in GitHub Actions, upload the production bundle to S3, and deliver it through CloudFront.
- Rationale: The authenticated SPA is static and does not need a permanent frontend server.

## D013: No AWS CodeBuild For Frontend

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Do not use AWS CodeBuild for the OrganicSEOOps frontend deployment.
- Rationale: The user explicitly selected GitHub Actions as the build and deployment runner.

## D014: No Initial Angular SSR

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: The authenticated application is a client-rendered SPA with `noindex`; it does not initially use SSR or prerendering.
- Rationale: Private dashboard pages do not need search indexing. A public marketing site can be handled separately later.

## D015: Background Jobs

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Use Celery with Redis for long-running jobs and PostgreSQL JobRun records as the user-visible source of truth.
- Rationale: Imports, crawling, audits, web research, and AI work should not hold HTTP requests open.

## D016: Explicit Job Catalog

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Add explicit code-defined job handlers and structured artifacts rather than an initial visual workflow builder.
- Rationale: This provides the required extensibility without building a speculative general-purpose orchestration product.

## D017: Deterministic Analysis First

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Calculate objective SEO, performance, and analytics findings with code and SQL. Use AI for interpretation, grouping, research, prioritization, and drafting.
- Rationale: Metrics should be reproducible and auditable.

## D018: Configurable AI Routing

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Configure provider and model choices by job type, with project and individual-job overrides, fallback, budgets, and usage tracking.
- Rationale: Classification, research, and final writing do not require the same model quality or cost.

## D019: Initial AI Adapters

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Begin implementation with native OpenAI support and an OpenAI-compatible adapter, then add native provider adapters when needed.
- Rationale: This creates practical flexibility without implementing every provider before product validation.

## D020: Free Models Are Opt-In

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Free or contributor-tier models are not automatic defaults for confidential jobs.
- Rationale: Retention, training, rate limits, and reliability may be inappropriate for customer and business data.

## D021: No Initial Vector Database

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Use PostgreSQL and ordinary search/query capabilities initially.
- Rationale: The agreed workflows do not yet demonstrate a vector database requirement.

## D022: Evidence And Citations

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Recommendations and research must preserve supporting metrics and sources. Web citations shown to users must be visible and clickable.
- Rationale: OrganicSEOOps should be an evidence-backed operating tool, not an opaque content generator.

## D023: Human Publication Approval

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Generated content and remediation remain drafts until an authorized human approves them.
- Rationale: This prevents unsupported claims, brand mistakes, and unsafe automated changes.

## D024: Trend Sources

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Combine project queries, official Google Trends access when available, YouTube, RSS, news, and other project-relevant sources. Do not depend on unofficial Trends scraping.
- Rationale: No single API reliably represents all daily relevant trends.

## D025: Competitor Data Labels

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Label third-party traffic, keyword, backlink, and authority metrics as estimates.
- Rationale: Competitor data is not first-party measurement.

## D026: Security Scope

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Start with passive security observations on authorized domains. Defer active scanning.
- Rationale: Active scanning carries legal, safety, and operational risk and requires explicit verification and authorization.

## D027: Subscription Timing

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Design ownership and usage around organizations but do not build billing or empty subscription tables initially.
- Rationale: The future requirement is known, but actual plans and entitlements should be based on product usage.

## D028: OpenCode Scope

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Defer OpenCode integration to a repository-remediation phase using isolated execution and pull-request review.
- Rationale: OpenCode is useful for code fixes, not as the core SEO research or content API.

## D029: Documentation-Only Checkpoint

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: The initial filesystem change contains project documentation only, with no Angular, Django, Docker, deployment, or runtime scaffold.
- Rationale: Preserve the complete plan before implementation begins, as explicitly requested.

## D030: Skills And Subagents Timing

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Do not create speculative project skills and subagents before real source paths and verification commands exist.
- Rationale: `AGENTS.md`, the canonical plan, and the persistent TODO are sufficient for the documentation checkpoint. Skills become useful when they can reference actual workflows.

## D031: Provider-Neutral Agent Resources

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Keep shared rules, Agent Skills-compatible workflows, and references under `.agents/`. Use `AGENTS.md` as the common root instruction file and thin provider adapters only where discovery differs. A documentation-maintenance skill is allowed now; implementation specialists remain deferred until source paths and verification commands exist.
- Rationale: OpenCode and Codex CLI discover `AGENTS.md` and `.agents/skills/`, while Claude Code can import the same root guidance through `CLAUDE.md`. This keeps one maintainable source without pretending every tool auto-loads arbitrary rule and reference paths.
- Replaces: D030 only to the extent that a concrete documentation-maintenance skill is now permitted during the documentation checkpoint; its ban on speculative implementation skills and subagents remains in effect.

## D032: Targeted Crawling And External SEO Coverage

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Crawl project sites and explicitly selected competitors rather than the whole web. Use external providers for broader backlink coverage, keyword discovery and volume/difficulty data, scheduled SERP rank observations, and optional competitor traffic estimates. Preserve dated snapshots, provider provenance, coverage gaps, and estimate labels; do not claim Semrush/Ahrefs parity from targeted crawling alone.
- Rationale: A site crawl reveals its public content and outgoing links, not its complete inbound links, search demand, rankings, actual traffic, or pre-existing history. Combining targeted crawling, authorized first-party analytics, and external datasets supports the intended workflows without operating a global index.
- Clarifies D025: Label estimated metrics as estimates and authority/difficulty as provider-specific scores. Individual backlink and SERP records are coverage-limited observations; third-party origin alone does not make every record an estimate.

## D033: Multi-Engine Webmaster Integration Growth

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Start with Google Search Console and plan Bing Webmaster Tools, Yandex Webmaster, and demand-driven additional connectors using shared project-scoped integration and job infrastructure. Keep provider/property identity, capabilities, metric semantics, credentials, checkpoints, quotas, and freshness explicit. Normalize comparable data without erasing engine-specific behavior; verify supported APIs and access at implementation time.
- Rationale: Projects may need several search engines concurrently. Source-aware storage, dashboards, detectors, and independently scheduled syncs let new connectors and worker capacity be added without replacing the data platform or conflating incompatible metrics.

## D034: Actionable SEO And Outcome Measurement

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Add intent/keyword-to-page mapping; assigned actions, change logging, verification, and outcome tracking; deeper crawl diagnostics; indexation reconciliation and optional authorized bot logs; local SEO; manually managed authority/link-recovery opportunities; content quality/lifecycle management; and analytics-quality review. Reuse existing project, opportunity, content, integration, and job infrastructure.
- Priority: After foundational data collection, emphasize page/action mapping, change/outcome tracking, and deeper crawl/indexation diagnostics. Establish analytics-quality context with integrations and assess local SEO early for the initial projects. Add richer local/provider and optional log integrations in the expanded integration phase.
- Rationale: The product should explain what to fix, why, and what changed afterward, rather than only producing reports or new articles. Measurement must expose seasonality, data gaps, attribution limits, and concurrent changes; before/after differences alone do not prove causation.
- Boundaries: Keep implementation, approval, verification, and outcomes distinct. Local profiles require actual eligibility, content needs genuine business value, and outreach remains manual. Content removals and redirects require review; crawl success and bot visits do not prove indexing.
- Updates: Opportunity assignment and change tracking are now planned parts of the initial opportunity release rather than optional unspecified later work. Targeted rendering can also serve explicitly requested raw-versus-rendered diagnostics under crawl budgets.

## D035: Portable Planning Reviewers With OpenCode Adapters

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Add read-only `seo-reviewer` and `plan-reviewer` roles during the documentation checkpoint. Keep their substantive instructions provider-neutral in `.agents/roles/`, with thin `.opencode/agents/` Markdown adapters for discovery and read/search/public-documentation-fetch permissions. Deny edits, shell commands, nested delegation, and unlisted tools; inherit the invoking agent's model. The main agent/user handles decisions and changes.
- Rationale: SEO and architecture/documentation review have concrete inputs now. Shared Markdown can be reused by other hosts, but subagent registration and permission enforcement are tool-specific; OpenCode adapters provide actual callable subagents without duplicating project knowledge or creating a universal orchestration framework.
- Replaces: D030 and D031 only where they defer all specialist subagents. These two planning reviewers are now permitted; speculative implementation skills and coding specialists remain deferred until real source paths and verification commands exist.

## D036: Complete Public SaaS Journey And Commercial Readiness

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Include public marketing/pricing/demo/help/trust pages, secure account lifecycle edge cases, an explicit role/action matrix, organization-admin and platform-support workflows, progressive onboarding, accessible consistent UI, reports/notifications/support, and export/closure/retention. Automatically grant one eligible organization trial after verified-email organization creation. Implement subscription lifecycle, server-side entitlements/usage limits, hosted payment flows, and verified/idempotent billing-event handling before public trial/paid launch.
- Rationale: Customers need a reliable path from discovery to first value, payment, ongoing use, and exit. Billing and role checks must constrain expensive background work, not just hide UI controls. Trial activation must be idempotent and separate from consent to future charges.
- Delivery: Internal SEO validation may precede commercial readiness. Staff MFA and the complete public journey are public-launch requirements; the dashboard remains a private client-rendered SPA while public marketing pages are independently indexable. Target WCAG 2.2 AA with meaningful usability/accessibility checks.
- Open decisions: Seven-day versus thirty-day trial, card requirement and renewal consent, prices/allowances, repeat-organization eligibility, billing provider/version, grace/proration/refund/tax policies, retention durations, and customer MFA rollout are not yet selected. Record these before public offers go live.
- Replaces: D027's late subscription timing; billing/trials are now a commercial-readiness milestone independent of scale. D014's no-SSR dashboard decision remains; public marketing pages are now required for public launch. Documentation-only status and the ban on empty/speculative implementation scaffolding remain.

## D037: Evidence-Labeled AI Visibility

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Add bounded, versioned prompt-based answer observations through supported APIs/providers or labeled manual observations, brand/domain/competitor mentions and citations, like-for-like history, identifiable AI-referral analytics, and discoverability recommendations. Reuse project-scoped jobs, provider capabilities, citations, snapshots, and budgets. Compute metrics from explicit eligible samples and expose failures, coverage, methodology, and collection surface.
- Rationale: Customers want to understand AI discovery alongside traditional search, but sampled answers cannot represent every private conversation and API output is not equivalent to consumer-app output. Crawler access, model training, answer citations, and referral traffic are separate signals.
- Boundaries: No consumer-account automation/access bypass, global chat market-share claims, inferred referrals from mentions, or guaranteed inclusion promises. Distinguish search, training, and user-triggered crawler controls using current provider documentation. Exact first platforms, sample budgets, and cadence remain implementation-time decisions.

## D038: Explicit Models For Planning Reviewers

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Pin `seo-reviewer` to `openai/gpt-5.6-terra` and `plan-reviewer` to `openai/gpt-5.6-sol`, both with `variant: high`, in their OpenCode adapters. Keep shared role instructions provider-neutral and credentials outside the repository.
- Rationale: The user requested explicit per-agent models as used in the sibling TransferTakip and AirportTransfer projects. Follow AirportTransfer's SEO and critical-review assignments; both model identifiers are available in the local OpenCode catalog.
- Replaces: D035 only where it requires inheriting the invoking agent's model. Existing read-only permissions and explicit opt-in for free models remain in effect.

## D039: Custom Public Pages With Static Prerendering And CSR

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Build public landing/marketing pages with semantic Angular HTML templates and custom native CSS, without PrimeNG or other UI/CSS frameworks. Prerender indexable public pages at build time; use CSR for account/authenticated application routes. Deploy static frontend artifacts to S3/CloudFront with no request-time SSR or production Node frontend server. Node/build-time rendering tools are allowed in development and CI.
- Rationale: This supports elegant, purpose-designed public pages and crawlable initial HTML while retaining simple static hosting and an interactive Angular dashboard. PrimeNG remains a candidate for authenticated controls, with lazy-loaded/scoped dependencies rather than public-page styling.
- Delivery requirements: Enumerate public routes, publish their complete HTML/metadata, retain true public 404s and HTTP redirects, scope CSR fallback to account/app routes, and refresh all generated HTML when public content changes. Verify build/CDN behavior and performance budgets instead of assuming prerendering alone guarantees SEO or speed.
- Clarifies: D012 and D014's static hosting/CSR direction and D036's public-page scope. Marketing rendering/styling technology is now selected; URL layout, content authoring workflow, exact versions, and visual design remain implementation decisions.

## D040: Concrete Launch Specifications And Readiness Evidence

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Require a selected launch audience/value proposition, a public page/keyword/intent map, truthful sample/proof material, an owned marketing content preview/review/build/release workflow, explicit language/market coverage, acquisition-to-revenue measurement definitions, and first-party website SEO/provider-readiness checks. Add these to the existing commercial-readiness and deployment work rather than creating another product module.
- Rationale: Page categories and a large feature plan are insufficient to define what customers will see, what promises can be supported, how public content stays current, and how launch results will be measured. The product's own website needs verified SEO and analytics behavior in production.
- Boundaries: Preserve custom HTML/CSS public pages, build-time prerendering, CSR account/app routes, and static hosting (D039). Demo results must be labeled; case studies need real evidence/permission. Localization is conditional on selected languages, and product analytics stays separate from customer website data. Marketing content publication does not wait for customer CMS integrations.
- Open choices: Audience, actual URLs/queries, markets/languages, publishing owners/content source, analytics/consent tooling and attribution windows, provider selections/budgets, and monitoring cadence are not yet chosen. Record concrete choices and readiness evidence before the related public promises launch; a supported static/repository-based workflow is sufficient unless an editing need justifies a CMS.

## D041: Angular 22 And Django 6.1 Release Lines

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Use Angular 22 for the frontend and Django 6.1 for the backend. At scaffold time, select the latest stable compatible releases within those lines, use a matching Angular CLI and compatible Django REST Framework, verify dependency compatibility against official documentation, and record exact version pins here.
- Rationale: The user accepted these current stable framework lines for the new project, making the implementation targets explicit while allowing patch updates before scaffolding.
- Replaces: D008 only where it leaves the Angular and Django release lines entirely open until scaffold time. D039's exact-version deferral now applies within these selected lines; other dependency versions remain scaffold-time choices. Any change to the selected release lines requires a new recorded decision.
- References: [Angular releases](https://angular.dev/reference/releases), [Angular compatibility](https://angular.dev/reference/versions), [Django releases](https://www.djangoproject.com/download/), and [DRF requirements](https://www.django-rest-framework.org/#requirements).

## D042: Detailed Implementation Tasks And Cross-Model Continuity

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Maintain `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_GUIDE.md` for provider-neutral coding/resume instructions, `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_TASKS.md` for stable task IDs, dependencies, coding steps and acceptance checks, and `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATE.md` for durable authorization/scope, active substeps, actual paths/commands, verification evidence, incomplete work, blockers and the exact next action. Route these documents from the root guidance and document map; keep the canonical plan authoritative and TODO as the phase-level backlog.
- Rationale: The user requested detailed coding instructions derived from the project's documents/rules/references/skills/reviewer guidance and the ability to continue interrupted implementation with the same or a different model. Repository-resident state and verification evidence provide continuity without depending on conversation history or a particular host.
- Workflow: Record the active slice before substantial edits, checkpoint after meaningful code/check slices and before handoffs, and reconcile actual files/migrations/processes/external effects when resuming. Unknown outcomes require reconciliation rather than blind retries. Only verified completion updates task/phase completion; interrupted/unverified work remains explicit. Keep handoffs secret-free and accepted product changes synchronized across plan, decisions and backlog.
- Boundaries: This decision prepares execution documents only; application implementation remains unstarted until explicitly requested. It does not select unresolved commercial/provider/version choices, initialize Git, authorize commits/deployments, or create speculative implementation skills/agents. Existing reviewers remain read-only and continuity does not depend on their availability.

## D043: Affordable GPT Model For Exploration

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Assign the built-in OpenCode `explore` subagent to `openai/gpt-5.4-mini` with `variant: low` in project `opencode.json`, preserving its built-in prompt and permissions.
- Rationale: The user requested an affordable GPT model instead of inheriting the primary model. File discovery, reference tracing, and codebase summaries are suitable bounded tasks for a smaller model with low reasoning effort.
- Validation: The local OpenCode catalog includes this model and maps its `low` variant to `reasoningEffort: low`. Configuration validation does not establish measured cost savings or workload quality.
- Reference: [GPT-5.4 Mini model documentation](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/gpt-5.4-mini).

## D044: Luna For Exploration

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Assign the built-in OpenCode `explore` subagent to `openai/gpt-5.6-luna`, retaining `variant: low` in project `opencode.json`.
- Rationale: The user requested at least Luna for exploration after the initial GPT-5.4 Mini assignment.
- Validation: The local OpenCode catalog includes `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` and maps its `low` variant to `reasoningEffort: low`.
- Replaces: D043's model selection only; the built-in exploration prompt and permissions are retained.

## D045: Six Read-Only Specialists And Dynamic Complex-Review Models

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Add `django-tenancy-reviewer`, `angular-ui-reviewer`, `jobs-integrations-reviewer`, `billing-entitlements-reviewer`, `security-reviewer`, and `deployment-reviewer` now, with provider-neutral instructions under `.agents/roles/` and thin OpenCode adapters. All six and `plan-reviewer` inherit the invoking primary agent's current model by omitting `model`; omit fixed `variant` overrides for cross-model compatibility. Keep `seo-reviewer` on `openai/gpt-5.6-terra`/`high` and `explore` on `openai/gpt-5.6-luna`/`low`.
- Rationale: The user explicitly requested all six recommended reviewers during the documentation checkpoint and selected their current, changeable model for complex work rather than a fixed model identifier.
- Boundaries: Reviewers allow project reading/search and public official documentation fetching, with edits, shell/tests, operational APIs, active scanning, and nested delegation prohibited. Review plans before source exists; later inspect actual source/check evidence without inventing paths or claiming tests ran. The main agent applies changes and runs checks. This tooling request does not start application implementation or authorize Git/deployment operations.
- Replaces: D030, D031, D035, and D042 only where they defer these six read-only specialist roles; implementation skills and write-capable coding specialists remain deferred until real source patterns and commands exist. Replaces D038's fixed model/variant for `plan-reviewer` only; its SEO assignment remains current.
- Reference: [OpenCode model inheritance](https://opencode.ai/docs/agents/#model). Model inheritance is resolved on invocation, not pinned to the model used while authoring the adapters.

## D046: Fixed Sol Model For Complex Reviewers

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Pin `plan-reviewer`, `django-tenancy-reviewer`, `angular-ui-reviewer`, `jobs-integrations-reviewer`, `billing-entitlements-reviewer`, `security-reviewer`, and `deployment-reviewer` to `openai/gpt-5.6-sol` with `variant: high` in their OpenCode adapters. Keep `seo-reviewer` on `openai/gpt-5.6-terra`/`high` and `explore` on `openai/gpt-5.6-luna`/`low`.
- Rationale: The user replaced current-model inheritance with fixed routing and explicitly selected Sol/high for all seven complex reviewers. Their routing should remain stable when the primary agent's model changes.
- Validation: `opencode models openai --verbose` lists Sol and maps its `high` variant to `reasoningEffort: high`; this establishes local configuration support, not measured review quality or successful live inference.
- Replaces: D045 only where it specifies omitted model/variant fields and dynamic inheritance. Its six specialist roles, read-only boundaries, provider-neutral shared instructions, and documentation-only project status remain in effect.

## D047: Routine Angular Reviews On Luna And Targeted Specialist Use

- Status: Accepted
- Decision: Pin `angular-ui-reviewer` to `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` with `variant: low` for routine UI review. Keep `plan-reviewer`, Django/tenancy, jobs/integrations, billing/entitlements, security, and deployment reviewers on Sol/high; SEO remains Terra/high and exploration remains Luna/low.
- Invocation: Use bounded specialist reviews only when requested or relevant to a substantive risk/decision; do not run the full roster after every change. The main agent handles routine consistency checks. Reserve deeper reviews for architecture decisions, authorization/data isolation, billing/concurrency, job reliability, security boundaries, and production/recovery milestones.
- Escalation: Complex Angular rendering, authentication, accessibility, or unresolved cross-layer issues go back to the parent with evidence and a specific question. The parent may route that scope to the existing Sol/high plan reviewer with the Angular role criteria, or the appropriate security/tenancy/deployment reviewer. Reviewers remain unable to delegate; no extra agents or automatic model-routing system are introduced.
- Rationale: The user accepted a lower-cost routine Angular assignment and selective use of deeper reviewers. These are workload-based defaults, not measured model-quality or cost guarantees.
- Validation: The local OpenCode catalog lists Luna and maps its `low` variant to `reasoningEffort: low`.
- Replaces: D046's Angular model/variant only; fixed routing, read-only boundaries, and all other model assignments remain. Agent-tooling changes do not authorize application implementation.

## D048: Begin Local Implementation Through The Continuation Workflow

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-21
- Decision: The user's explicit request to code following CONTINUE.md starts local implementation at the first eligible task, P01-01, with bounded verified slices and durable checkpoints.
- Scope: Begin Phase 1 workspace/version verification and dependency-ready scaffolding. Git initialization/commits, deployment and paid provider operations require their own authorization. Unresolved product choices remain explicit.
- Replaces: D029's documentation-only stopping point and D042's unstarted status; their documentation-preservation and continuity requirements remain. D041's release lines and stable-compatibility gate remain mandatory.

## D049: Initial Compatible Scaffold Pins And Package Managers

- Status: Accepted — official documentation and publisher registry metadata checked 2026-09-21 (P01-01).
- Decision: Retain D041's release lines with the following exact scaffold pins. Install only dependencies used by the active task; later service pins are not claims of completed integration tests.

| Component | Pin | Compatibility evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Angular framework/compiler | 22.1.7 | Latest stable publisher npm tag; core requires matching compiler and RxJS `^6.5.3 || ^7.4.0` |
| Angular CLI/build/SSR tooling | 22.1.8 | Latest stable tags; peers support Angular `^22.0.0`; independent framework/tooling patch numbers are expected |
| Node / npm | 24.21.0 / 11.19.0 | Latest Node 24 LTS release and bundled npm; Angular tooling permits Node `^24.15.0` |
| TypeScript / RxJS / tslib | 6.0.3 / 7.8.2 / 2.8.1 | Angular compiler/build requires TypeScript `>=6.0 <6.1`; latest TypeScript 7.0.2 is therefore excluded |
| Python | 3.13.15 | Latest micro in 3.13; supported by Django 6.1, DRF, Celery and psycopg publisher metadata |
| Django / DRF | 6.1.1 / 3.18.1 | Django's latest official stable release; DRF requirements explicitly list Django 6.1 and Python 3.13 |
| PostgreSQL / psycopg | 18.6 / 3.3.6 | Latest stable PostgreSQL 18 maintenance release; Django 6.1 requires PostgreSQL 15+ and psycopg 3.1.12+ |
| Celery / Kombu | 5.6.3 / 5.6.2 | Stable releases; Celery requires Kombu 5.6+ and supports Django integration; Python 3.13 supported |
| Redis server / Python client | 8.10.2 / 6.4.0 | Stable Redis release; redis-py 6+ supports Redis 7.2 to current; Kombu Redis extra requires redis-py `>=4.5.2,<6.5` with two excluded older patches |
| pip | 26.2.1 | Stable publisher release, Python 3.10+ |

- Package managers: npm with exact direct pins and a generated `package-lock.json`, reproducible installs via `npm ci`; Python virtual environment/container with pip, exact direct requirements and a resolved transitive requirements lock. Use pinned Python containers for backend verification rather than modifying the system Python. The existing host Node 24.16.0 is compatible; use the selected Node/npm pair via isolated npm execution for reproducible frontend checks.
- Sources: [Angular compatibility](https://angular.dev/reference/versions), [Angular release policy](https://angular.dev/reference/releases), publisher metadata via `npm view` for core/CLI/build/SSR/compiler-cli/TypeScript/RxJS/tslib, [Node release index](https://nodejs.org/dist/index.json), [Python releases](https://www.python.org/downloads/), [Django downloads](https://www.djangoproject.com/download/), [Django Python compatibility](https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.1/faq/install/), [Django PostgreSQL requirements](https://docs.djangoproject.com/en/6.1/ref/databases/#postgresql-notes), [DRF requirements](https://www.django-rest-framework.org/#requirements), [PostgreSQL support](https://www.postgresql.org/support/versioning/), [Celery introduction](https://docs.celeryq.dev/en/stable/getting-started/introduction.html), [Celery Django integration](https://docs.celeryq.dev/en/stable/django/first-steps-with-django.html), [Redis release](https://github.com/redis/redis/releases/tag/8.10.2), [redis-py compatibility](https://github.com/redis/redis-py#supported-redis-versions), and publisher PyPI JSON metadata for Django/DRF/Celery/Kombu/redis/psycopg/pip.
- Evidence nuance: Angular's compatibility table currently labels its 22 row `22.0.x`; 22.1.7 compiler and 22.1.8 build metadata independently confirm the selected Node/TypeScript/peer ranges. Celery's introductory text retains some 5.5 wording; 5.6.3 package metadata confirms the Python and dependency constraints used here. Resolver/build/runtime checks remain mandatory in the owning scaffold tasks.

## D052: Local Service Definitions And Backend Serving Dependencies

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-21, P01-04 implementation detail within the approved separate-service architecture. Runtime/container acceptance remains deferred under D051.

Reuse D049's Python/PostgreSQL/Redis/Celery/Kombu/redis-py pins. Exact Python transitive pins are resolved in the backend lock.

PostgreSQL, Redis AOF and Beat state use named volumes; migrations are explicit, not concurrent startup side effects.

This is runnable transport configuration, not job reliability/entitlement implementation.

YAML parsing/source-contract checks are not Compose configuration validation, image builds, real Redis/proxy networking, restart persistence or production acceptance. nginx/Redis image tags remain unpulled/unverified until the user lifts D051. Loopback HTTP and private unauthenticated Redis are local-only; production TLS/Redis access controls and deployment checks remain later work.
- Evidence: publisher PyPI metadata for Gunicorn/WhiteNoise/Celery/Kombu confirms Python compatibility and dependency bounds; [Gunicorn](https://gunicorn.org/), [WhiteNoise Django integration](https://whitenoise.readthedocs.io/en/stable/django.html), [Celery Django integration](https://docs.celeryq.dev/en/stable/django/first-steps-with-django.html), and [nginx stable downloads](https://nginx.org/en/download.html) reviewed on 2026-09-21.

## D055: Minimal Check-Only CI And Vulnerability Gates

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-21, P01-05 implementation detail; execution results remain in the checkpoint.
- Decision: Add GitHub Actions checks on push, pull request and manual dispatch with read-only contents permission and no deployment. Pin checkout v7.0.1 to commit `3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1`, disable persisted credentials, and use the target Node 24.21.0/npm 11.19.0 container for frontend checks. Backend checks build/run the existing target Python/PostgreSQL Compose configuration in an isolated `organicseoops-ci` project with generated ephemeral credentials. Make Beat's singleton name project-scoped so CI and stopped development resources cannot collide.
- Tool pins: actionlint 1.7.12 (image digest `sha256:b1934ee5f1c509618f2508e6eb47ee0d3520686341fec936f3b79331f9315667`), Trivy 0.74.0 (image digest `sha256:62b1e65e8869bc4b4c6aa4fa2b21595256c7c2f6018a9d9ad61caf87187c1969`), and PyYAML 6.0.3 in an isolated check-only virtualenv. Node image digest is `sha256:0e0ff40c39bc087845bfb27465a0df4ea419520094bc35842ff83dd8cbe6f9b6`. No check tool becomes an application dependency.
- Security checks: native npm audit includes development dependencies; Trivy vulnerability-only scans cover the backend lock and four runtime images (shared backend, PostgreSQL, Redis, nginx). Fail on HIGH/CRITICAL findings, including unfixed ones, or scanner/database errors. Do not silently add ignores, `continue-on-error`, or broad allowlists. The threshold is a baseline CI failure policy, not a claim that lower-severity findings or scanner coverage gaps are harmless.
- Scanner boundary: export the actual running Compose image IDs to temporary archives and scan those read-only, rather than giving Trivy the Docker socket. This avoids claiming a read-only socket mount enforces a read-only Docker API and ties reports to the image set actually tested.
- D054 remains local-only. Its Redis exception does not suppress vulnerability findings or waive security-patch restoration before production. A failed/unrun required scan leaves P01-05 unfinished even when application tests pass.
- Rationale: reuse the actual npm/Django/Compose checks, a small maintained workflow linter and one container/lockfile scanner. Keep scripts, credentials and local/hosted evidence distinct; there is no Git repository/remote yet, so local command validation does not imply a hosted Actions run.
- Evidence: publisher release metadata for [checkout v7.0.1](https://github.com/actions/checkout/releases/tag/v7.0.1), [Trivy v0.74.0](https://github.com/aquasecurity/trivy/releases/tag/v0.74.0), [actionlint v1.7.12](https://github.com/rhysd/actionlint/releases/tag/v1.7.12), PyPI PyYAML metadata, checkout node24 action metadata, [Trivy filesystem scans](https://trivy.dev/latest/docs/target/filesystem/) and [actionlint usage](https://github.com/rhysd/actionlint/blob/v1.7.12/docs/usage.md) checked. Exact image pulls succeeded; vulnerability database acquisition and scan results are separate evidence.

## D056: Minimal Alpine Runtime Images And Prepare-Only Database Transition

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-21. User selected “Prepare and verify only”: adopt image definitions, rehearse logical restore on isolated fixtures, and leave the running development stack/data untouched. Do not migrate/restart that stack without later authorization.

Install/check dependencies in a build-only environment; runtime images contain no pip/ensurepip installer. PostgreSQL starts directly as UID/GID70, so its unused gosu root-switch helper is removed; upstream entrypoint supports nonroot initialization. API/workers remain UID10001 and share one image.

Initialize new databases with PostgreSQL's builtin C.UTF-8 locale provider and UTF8 encoding; moving existing data requires logical dump/restore, rebuilding indexes under the selected provider, verification and preserved rollback data. This database locale is not a product language/market choice.

Full repository-image functional/security checks and logical-restore rehearsal remain mandatory before P01-05 completion. No vulnerability ignore or policy waiver is introduced.
- Isolation: allow a configurable loopback proxy port and project name in check-env generation so verification can coexist with the user's active stack. Development defaults remain loopback8080. Pin the already verified Redis8.10.1/nginx1.30.5 base digests; D054's Redis security-patch restoration remains a production prerequisite.
- Replaces: Bookworm container-base implementation and runtime-pip-check placement only; framework release lines, PostgreSQL as data store, container separation and D055 gate remain. Build-time native pip dependency checks plus runtime locked-version checks replace a runtime installer dependency. Local non-container venv instructions may still use pip normally.
- Evidence: official Python/PostgreSQL image catalogs and Dockerfiles, PostgreSQL [entrypoint](https://github.com/docker-library/postgres/blob/master/docker-entrypoint.sh), [initdb locale-provider options](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/app-initdb.html), published psycopg-binary3.3.6 CPython3.13 musllinux wheels, and candidate scan results recorded in the checkpoint. Existing development data migration is expressly unperformed.
- Verification outcome: canonical prepared images passed10 backend tests,7 check-tool tests, live queues/proxy/persistence, guarded synthetic logical restore with deliberate late-error atomic rollback/retry, and the unchanged vulnerability gate (exit0). Read-only startup guard was added to CI/documented startup; check profiles default to18080. The original9 development containers/IDs/start times remain unchanged. Local P01-05 acceptance is complete; D054's production requirement and actual user migration remain explicit follow-ups.

## D057: Minimal Structured Logging And Private Artifact Foundation

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-21, P01-06 implementation.
- Decision: Use stdlib JSON stdout logging with validated/generated request IDs, URL-free completion events, safe framework-path replacement and centralized cookie/auth/OAuth/CSRF/signed-URL redaction. Reapply the configuration through Celery's logging signal. Provider integrations must log fixed event/error categories rather than raw payloads; regex redaction is defense in depth.

S3 mode uses the default IAM credential chain, a dedicated private bucket/prefix, no public ACL/custom domain, authenticated five-minute URLs, no overwrite, AES256 and bounded spooling. No static AWS credential setting is added.

No Artifact model, public endpoint, presigned share or ownership claim is added before P02-03/P04-01. Real bucket policy/Block Public Access/IAM/lifecycle/anonymous-denial evidence remains P13-01; local configuration is not deployed S3 readiness.
- Evidence: django-storages S3/Django6.1 storage documentation and PyPI metadata checked. Isolated target image,20 backend tests,7 check-tool tests, full runtime storage/log probes, public-build secret marker scan and unchanged HIGH/CRITICAL gate passed. ## D059: Maintained Browser Account Primitive

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-21, P02-01 implementation.
- Decision: Pin `django-allauth[headless]`65.19.4 for email registration, mandatory verification, login/logout, browser sessions and account rate limiting. Enable only the browser headless client; do not add JWT, session-token headers, social login or local-storage authentication. Retain the custom email-only user model and normalize the whole email address in a thin allauth backend subclass.
- Browser boundary: Use same-origin credentialed requests, Secure/HttpOnly/SameSite=Lax session cookies outside DEBUG, a readable CSRF cookie submitted as `X-CSRFToken`, validated frontend/trusted origins and one trusted overwritten proxy hop. Store verification records in PostgreSQL so safe pending/expired/used/invalid UI states are available; put bearer keys in frontend URL fragments and remove them from history immediately.
- Abuse/privacy boundary: Combine per-address and per-source verification-mail limits, keep resend and authentication failures generic, never create organizations/trials in verification callbacks, and treat logout as complete only after an expected unauthenticated/success response. Console email is local-only; transactional delivery, timing equalization under a real provider and a production startup guard remain P3A-10.
- Evidence:25 PostgreSQL-backed backend tests,7 Angular interaction/route tests,3 static-output tests, exact Node24.21.0/npm11.19.0 build, 320px Chrome renders of registration/login/pending verification, nine-service target-runtime smoke, npm audit0 and unchanged HIGH/CRITICAL scans0 passed. Bounded security review `ses_f3ad70b14ffey0ndhbPJiJg6ix` and Angular review `ses_f3ad70a95ffejvQg9MLJdrHLUg` reported no remaining blocking P02-01 findings after remediation.

## D060: Isolated Source-Mounted Local Development

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-24, explicit user request for a reusable local/agent coding loop.
- Decision: Use an explicitly selected `compose.development.yaml` override and fixed `organicseoops-dev` project on loopback port18000. Keep its generated credentials, image tags, PostgreSQL/Redis/artifact/Beat volumes and networks separate from legacy and task-check projects. PostgreSQL/Redis keep no host port publication, so container `postgres:5432` is independent of host `127.0.0.1:5432`.
- Reload boundary: Mount only current backend application paths read-only into API/workers/Beat. Django `runserver` reloads API edits; Celery workers/Beat restart explicitly. Rebuild the dedicated backend dependency image only for requirement/Dockerfile changes, add mounts with future apps, and keep migrations explicit. Angular development proxies account/API paths to the isolated backend for same-origin session/CSRF behavior.
- Safety boundary: The wrapper names both Compose files/project/port, clears conflicting ambient interpolation values, guards database volume transitions and refuses credential regeneration when development state survives. It never migrates/recreates the legacy stack; `down` preserves named development volumes.
- Evidence: Dedicated backend/PostgreSQL images built from cache, all nine development services became healthy,31 PostgreSQL-backed tests and8 check-tool tests passed, and down/up retained migrations. Source mounts excluded the host virtualenv, API edits triggered Django reload, worker/Beat recreation left database/broker IDs unchanged, ShellCheck passed, and Angular `/api` plus `/_allauth` proxy requests reached the isolated backend. Host PostgreSQL remained on127.0.0.1:5432 while container PostgreSQL had no host binding. A runserver-only logger mismatch found during verification was fixed with path-redacted `django.server` coverage; bounded security/deployment reviews found no remaining blocker after hardening project/guard selection, migration generation and worker restart readiness.

## D061: Maintained Account Recovery And Session Controls

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-24, P02-02 implementation.
- Decision: Extend the pinned django-allauth browser-session integration rather than introducing custom reset-token or session models. Use allauth/Django for one-hour, single-use password reset; recent reauthentication; pending verified email changes and notices; tracked session listing/revocation; and account rate limits. Password reset and password change end all tracked sessions and require normal login again.
- Browser boundary: Put password-reset keys in URL fragments and remove them from browser history before exchange. Accept only the configured frontend origin, require same-origin cookie/CSRF requests, normalize changed addresses through the canonical user manager, keep recovery responses generic and expose explicit project endpoints only where allauth lacks the required response contract (`change-password` and server-authoritative `logout-all`).
- Delivery boundary: Console email remains local-only. Production sender authentication, delivery retries/bounces/complaints, real-provider timing review and startup safeguards remain P3A-10; this decision does not claim production email readiness.
- Evidence:31 PostgreSQL-backed backend tests cover known/unknown recovery, non-mutation, trusted fragment links, expiry/replay, session invalidation, notices, recent reauthentication, verified-only normalized email replacement, concurrent uniqueness and individual/logout-all revocation. Exact Node24.21.0/npm11.19.0 passed11 Angular interaction/route tests, the production build and3 static-output tests; 320px Chrome renders showed recovery/security reflow without visible clipping. Nine-service target-runtime smoke, npm audit0 and the unchanged HIGH/CRITICAL scanner gate with0 findings passed. Bounded security review `ses_f2e3e4066ffe18PGyMswTYqo5Z` reported no remaining blocker after remediation.

## D064: Organization And Project Tenancy Boundary

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-24, P02-03 implementation.
- Data boundary: Organizations own memberships and projects; projects own optional restricted-access membership and explicit domain aliases. Store the four accepted role values now, atomically create the first verified owner, and defer invitations, role mutation, ownership transfer/last-owner enforcement and suspension to P02-04/P02-05. Organization creation does not create or reset a trial or subscription.
- Authorization boundary: Customer access begins with fail-closed user-scoped organization/project querysets. Staff/superuser status grants no implicit customer access; inaccessible direct or nested IDs return404. Until P02-04, only owners can create projects or change basic organization/project settings. Private API responses and browser requests are no-store, and Angular role visibility is convenience rather than authorization.
- Domain/artifact boundary: Each project has one normalized exact HTTP(S) canonical origin plus explicit normalized aliases; transactional services serialize same-organization origin changes and reject canonical/alias collisions. Restricted projects require explicit project membership except for owners. Internal artifact wrappers accept only persisted project objects and exact organization/project namespaces; no Artifact model, endpoint, share, cache layer or crawl authorization is introduced.
- Frontend boundary: `/app` uses server-fed organization/project selectors with visible active context, immediate project-state clearing, delayed-response isolation, distinct loading/empty/error states with retry, verified-user organization creation and owner-only basic settings. It does not add team management, billing, trials or SEO operations.
- Evidence: Final image `sha256:653dfe7699ca23e3b5fb76d0c41d82bbbcbcb2fa7ef0faad6ff99db46cd5a596` passed system/current/drift checks and all44 PostgreSQL-backed account/config/tenancy tests on Python3.13.15/PostgreSQL18.6. Exact Node24.21.0/npm11.19.0 fresh install passed14 Angular tests, production build,3 static checks and audit0. Nine-service smoke,9 check-tool tests and the unchanged HIGH/CRITICAL scanner gate passed with0 findings. A320px Chrome owner-workspace render had no horizontal overflow. Django-tenancy, Angular and security reviews found no remaining actionable issue after remediation; residual bulk-operation, future caller authorization and real assistive-technology checks remain explicit later boundaries.

## D065: Explicit Role Actions, Digest Invitations And Audited Revocation

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-24, P02-04 implementation.
- Authorization boundary: Encode organization and project actions explicitly rather than ordering roles. Owners and admins may manage non-owner memberships and invitations within accessible project scope; owner-target mutation, ownership transfer, last-owner protection, reauthentication and suspension remain P02-05. Organization-only checks reject project actions, project checks require an authorized project, and later jobs/delivery/integrations must call the same checks independently of entitlements.
- Invitation boundary: Normalize the invited email, store only a SHA-256 token digest, put the bearer token in the frontend URL fragment, require the matching verified account, enforce server-time expiry and inviter authority at acceptance, and make acceptance replay-safe under concurrency. Revocation and attempted use of expired invitations durably close their pending state; invitations never create organizations, trials, subscriptions or entitlements.
- Audit/revocation boundary: Membership deletion is revocation. Role, membership, invitation and restricted-project access changes write bounded immutable audit events in the same transaction, including request IDs but no bearer tokens. Subsequent API operations re-evaluate membership; future worker/export/delivery stages retain their own revocation rechecks.
- Email configuration boundary: Production settings fail closed when `EMAIL_BACKEND` is absent or selects Django's console, in-memory, dummy or file backend. Compose forwards the explicitly configured value, production env generation leaves it blank, and the image build uses command-scoped SMTP only for `collectstatic`; no delivery credential or provider setting is baked into the image. P3A-10 still owns sender/provider selection, authenticated-domain setup, real delivery, retries, bounces and lifecycle communications.
- Evidence: Final image `sha256:5a4d06a319eff2829fd80158cb2d469a7471b96eae426f0248de86fcfec716d2` passed system/current/drift checks and all58 PostgreSQL-backed account/config/tenancy tests on Python3.13.15/PostgreSQL18.6. Exact Node24.21.0/npm11.19.0 passed19 Angular tests, production build,3 static checks and audit0. Nine deployment checks, nine-service smoke, ambient-environment isolation and the unchanged HIGH/CRITICAL scanner gate passed with0 findings. A320px team render had no horizontal overflow. Django-tenancy, Angular, security and deployment reviews found no remaining actionable issue after remediation.

## D066: Accepted Ownership Transfer, Last Owner And Reversible Suspension

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-24, P02-05 local implementation.
- Ownership boundary: A recently reauthenticated owner may propose a single pending transfer to an active, verified existing non-owner member. The separately reauthenticated recipient must explicitly accept; acceptance atomically promotes the recipient to owner, demotes the proposer to admin, preserves the proposer's previously accessible restricted projects, and records scoped audit events. Either participant can reauthenticate and cancel/decline; a cancelled transfer leaves both roles intact and frees the pending slot. Subsequent owner removal/demotion/leave and concurrent changes serialize on the organization row and cannot remove the last active owner. A leaving member's pending invitations are revoked.
- Suspension boundary: Staff actions require the relevant Django permission and a reason; org suspension hides project/team operations but leaves memberships and pending invitations intact. User suspension cannot disable the last active owner, revokes tracked sessions and rotates a per-user session-auth hash so even untracked existing Django sessions stay invalid after reactivation. Untouched users retain the historical session hash. Global user status events may have no organization; scoped organization events remain scoped. Suspension, closure and commercial expiry remain distinct.
- Delivery boundary: Customer workspace offers transfer proposal/acceptance/cancel, leave confirmation, and a clear suspended state; security settings explain closure prerequisites. Export/deletion/retention execution remains P02-06; billing/jobs/delivery rechecks belong to their owning cards. No email provider, commercial offer or hosted deployment is selected here.
- Evidence: `accounts.0002` and `tenancy.0004`–`0006` applied only to `organicseoops-dev`. Final target Python3.13.15/PostgreSQL18.6 source-mounted dev checks passed all69 `accounts config tenancy` tests, including concurrent owner changes, reauthentication, audit rollback, suspended invitation recovery and suspension/reactivation session revocation; Django system, current/drift and9 deployment-tool checks passed. Angular local `npm run check` passed21 interaction tests, production build and3 static checks. Earlier failed full Admin-add and suspension-reactivation runs were repaired and rerun; no production browser, hosted or provider acceptance is implied.

## D068: Persistent Sequential Multi-Card Continuation

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-24, user requested the recommended continuation behavior in CONTINUE.md rather than repeating a long prompt.
- Decision: Make `CONTINUE.md` the reusable entry point for sequential dependency-ready implementation within existing authorization. Finish partial work, verify each card, update its checkpoint/status, and immediately continue to the next eligible card. Use no fixed card limit by default; an explicit narrower user task/batch takes precedence. One primary active card limits concurrency, not cards per session.
- Stopping boundary: A completed card/checkpoint alone is not a reason to stop. Record blockers and continue independent authorized work when possible. Stop for requested scope completion/user pause, no remaining eligible authorized work, or an actual context/tool/session limit; record the specific reason and exact next action. No minimum card count is promised.
- Efficiency and scope: Reuse the development environment, already-loaded context and passing unaffected evidence. Load new/changed task context and run required affected checks and acceptance gates. This clarifies D042's continuation workflow without changing feature dependencies, security thresholds, implementation authorization, Git/deployment/paid-operation permissions or historical verification evidence.

## D069: Durable Job Records Before Dispatch

PostgreSQL stores authoritative status, ordered progress, input/config/method snapshots, output and failure category. Explicit legal transitions and immutable job identity/snapshots reject invalid changes; service and model validation reject unauthorized actors, cross-tenant project/job/citation/artifact links and unsafe artifact keys. No production handler, Celery dispatch, active scheduler, public job API, paid operation or unbounded work is introduced. P04-02 owns commit-safe/recoverable delivery and retries; P04-03 owns customer API/progress; P04-04 owns atomic usage/budgets; P04-05 owns Beat scheduling and worker-stage reauthorization. Dormant schedule records cannot be enabled before that work.

## D070: Commit-Safe Diagnostic Dispatch And Recoverable Redis Delivery

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-24, P04-02 local implementation.
- Dispatch boundary: An internal diagnostic request supplies a bounded idempotency key unique to project and handler. Persist the scoped queued job and immutable snapshots in PostgreSQL, publish only after the outer transaction commits, and send the job ID alone to the default Celery queue. Serialize sends with a database row lock; a broker exception leaves a recoverable queued row with a fixed non-sensitive failure category. A bounded explicit reconciler retries undispatched or stale queued sends, including unknown broker outcomes. The same key and unchanged actor/input/config return the same job; a conflicting reuse fails.
- Worker boundary: Late acknowledgment, bounded soft/hard timeouts and reject-on-worker-loss support redelivery. The worker locks the job during execution, so duplicate or terminal deliveries do not duplicate steps or other effects. The diagnostic handler is the only eligible task and remains DEBUG-only; no external provider side effect or paid operation can be blindly replayed. Revoked actors cancel protected queued work; unexpected handler errors record only a fixed category and terminal failed state. P04-03 adds customer job APIs/UI, P04-04 usage budgets, and P04-05 scheduler/stage recovery; the reconciler is callable manually until scheduling exists.
- Evidence: `jobs.0002` applied only to `organicseoops-dev`; four workers and Beat restarted healthy and all four workers registered `jobs.execute`. A synthetic dev fixture (marker `48d8f8d197b244dcb96e64e36668956c`) exercised complete/partial/duplicate real Redis/default-worker delivery; scoped user/org/project/jobs were cleaned and a read-only query found zero leftovers. `./backend-dev.sh test` passed78 PostgreSQL tests and Django system/current/drift; an additional crash-mid-step PostgreSQL test passed afterward, proving rollback to queued and successful stale reconciliation without repeated side effects. Nine source/check-tool tests passed. Both nine-service stacks remained running; no legacy migration, hosted CI or new image vulnerability scan is claimed.

## D071: Scoped Diagnostic Job API And Durable Cancellation Checkpoint

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-24, P04-03 local implementation.
- API boundary: Expose bounded, private/no-store project-scoped create/list/detail/cancel/retry command endpoints for the cost-free `diagnostic_probe` only while Django DEBUG is enabled. Nested organization/project/job IDs are resolved through current authenticated access and explicit role checks; CSRF protects mutations, viewers may read accessible jobs but cannot launch/cancel/retry, and staff status alone grants no customer scope. A retry creates or reuses a new job with a same-project `retry_of` link and stable idempotency key, preserving the original partial result. No production/paid job can be started by these endpoints.
- Cancellation boundary: Store a one-per-job, authorized `JobCancellation` request separately from the worker's status transaction. PostgreSQL NO KEY UPDATE row locks permit a running worker to observe that committed request between diagnostic stages without false terminal-state claims: queued work cancels when unlocked, a running request returns pending status, and work may finish the current stage before stopping. The diagnostic worker checks both cancellation and actor/project authorization after its first step, retaining completed evidence. Its two short stages otherwise commit atomically; long-running handlers need per-stage committed progress/leases under P04-05 (the `ponytail:` ceiling is marked in code).
- Browser boundary: A lazy Angular diagnostic route appears only in local development and shows visible project context, bounded recent jobs, zero-unit estimate, stage/partial/error/cancel-requested states, inspectable structured output, accessible status/error text, and retry/cancel controls. Polling stops at terminal state, navigation, denied scope or network failure; delayed old-project responses are ignored. Production builds do not link to this diagnostic and the backend responds404 outside DEBUG.
- Evidence: reviewed `jobs.0003`/`.0004` applied only to `organicseoops-dev`; dev workers/Beat restarted healthy after service changes. Final `./backend-dev.sh test` passed81 PostgreSQL-backed `accounts config tenancy jobs` tests plus system/current/drift; tests include tenant/role/CSRF/unknown-ID checks and a real two-connection pause-after-step HTTP202 cancellation followed by one retained step and terminal cancellation. Local `npm run check` passed25 Angular route/interaction tests, production build and3 static checks;9 deployment-tool checks passed. No P04-03 manual narrow-browser/assistive-technology, hosted CI, fresh-image vulnerability scan or live customer provider job is claimed.

## D072: Internal Unit Reservations And Concurrent Job Ceilings

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-24, P04-04 local implementation.
- Operational boundary: A job-owned `UsageReservation` records organization/project/provider key, UTC month, estimated internal units, actual units, reserved/settled/released state and settlement time. Serialize reserve/settle on the organization row; include active reservations and settled actual use in the monthly count, enforce organization/project/provider ceilings independently, and limit concurrent reserved slots even for zero-unit diagnostics. These units are not provider prices, customer credits or charges; public plan allowances and payment policy remain P3A-04/P3A-08 decisions. Initial **internal-only** monthly ceilings are100 organization,40 project and60 provider units; active job ceilings are4 organization,2 project and2 provider. Diagnostic jobs reserve0 units but occupy a slot; production-priced handlers remain disabled until provider capability/cost/budget validation.
- Settlement boundary: Reserve in the same transaction as queued-job creation, before post-commit dispatch. Terminal diagnostic success/partial/failure/cancellation releases its zero-unit reservation. Repeated settlement with the same actual use is idempotent; a changed repeat is refused, and actual use above the original estimate is recorded truthfully rather than hidden. A bounded reconciler releases only terminal zero-cost diagnostics; unknown-provider or still-running reservations remain held for operator/handler reconciliation. P04-05 owns recovery of genuinely stuck work and stage/schedule checks; external provider integrations must settle incurred usage even after partial failures.
- Evidence: `jobs.0005` applied only to `organicseoops-dev`, workers/Beat restarted. Four PostgreSQL usage tests cover independent caps, zero-unit concurrency, same-job settlement, synthetic over-estimate, unrefunded unknown-provider use and a two-thread quota race; full `./backend-dev.sh test` passed85 backend tests/system/current/drift. Compatible local Node `npm run check` passed25 Angular tests/build/3 static checks with a separate HTTP429 internal-capacity state. A uniquely marked synthetic dev fixture `528c40ff4ae9484da0053551e82ee0bb` completed normal/partial/duplicate real Redis work with both usage records released; owned cleanup ran and a follow-up query found zero matching users/orgs/projects/jobs/usage/cancellations. Both nine-service stacks remained running; no paid provider, hosted CI, fresh image scan or commercial policy activation occurred.

## D073: Bounded Diagnostic Schedules And Stuck-Job Recovery

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-24, P04-05 local implementation.
- Schedule boundary: Only the DEBUG-only cost-free diagnostic can be scheduled. A project-scoped, role-authorized schedule is created paused, with a bounded60–10080-minute interval and a unique request key. Explicit enable starts the next interval from server time; pause/renewed enable does not replay missed work. Singleton Beat enqueues a bounded due selector and recovery task on the default queue every60 seconds. The due selector locks the organization then schedule, emits at most one latest eligible occurrence, records bounded skipped-run history and deduplicates concurrently attempted occurrences with a database constraint. Revocation/suspension pauses the schedule; internal capacity defers it to the next interval with a visible fixed reason. Customer/API and inspect-only staff screens show next/last due, pause and error state without raw input payloads.
- Recovery/authorization boundary: Recheck current actor/project/org status and active internal capacity at publish time and after the first diagnostic stage. A revoked/blocked queued job cancels and settles its zero-unit reservation; a running diagnostic retains its completed first step if access or budget disappears. Recover only old diagnostic `running`/`waiting_provider` jobs whose row is not locked by live work, mark fixed-category failed and release known zero-use reservations; stale queued sends and terminal diagnostic reservations use the existing bounded reconcilers. Unknown provider use remains held for real reconciliation. Future external delivery, commercial entitlement checks and provider-specific retry/reconciliation are attached by P3A/P05/P08 owning cards; the diagnostic's atomic two-stage progress ceiling remains explicit.
- Evidence: reviewed `jobs.0006` applied only to `organicseoops-dev` after verifying no existing dev schedules and no migration drift; four workers and the single dev Beat restarted healthy. A synthetic due schedule with marker `100236db03d24d868bfdeb3b2d8a0fc8` produced one completed real Redis/default-worker job and released its zero-unit reservation; cleanup removed all seven matching resource types. PostgreSQL tests cover concurrent due selectors, missed-run collapse, idempotent schedule API, viewer denial, access loss, capacity deferral, over-budget dispatch, skip-locked stale recovery, stage-two suspension and Admin secret/edit denial. Final `./backend-dev.sh test` passed93 PostgreSQL tests/system/current/drift; local `npm run check` passed26 Angular tests/build/3 static checks;9 source checks and dev health200 passed. Both nine-service stacks stayed running. No paid provider, production schedule, hosted CI, fresh image scan or manual assistive-technology claim.

## D075: Maintained Staff TOTP With Session Assurance And Controlled Recovery

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-24, P03-02 local implementation.
- Authentication boundary: Extend pinned django-allauth65.19.4 with its maintained MFA app and browser APIs. Enable only TOTP and one-time recovery codes for staff; hide authenticator management from customers, disable trusted-browser bypass, and leave customer MFA/WebAuthn as separate future choices. Production Admin and `/api/platform/` operations require both active TOTP enrollment and an MFA authentication record in the current session, not merely a factor row. Unenrolled staff retain only the enrollment path; stale password-only staff sessions are signed out before privileged access. Django Admin login routes through allauth's rate-limited login rather than its standalone password form.
- Secret/session boundary: Store allauth TOTP and recovery seeds through versioned MultiFernet adapter hooks. Production requires separately managed comma-separated `MFA_ENCRYPTION_KEYS`, newest first; development derives a local-only key from its existing secret. Enrollment and recovery-code replacement rotate the user's session version, retain the verified current session, end tracked sibling sessions and write secret-free audit events. Authenticator data is inspect-only without secret fields in Admin. A different active superuser may run the reasoned, target-confirmed `reset_staff_mfa` management command after lost-factor authorization; it removes factors, rotates session state, ends tracked sessions, audits the operator/target/reason and requires fresh enrollment. No impersonation or operator-visible replacement secret is introduced.
- Browser/operations boundary: Angular login completes the allauth MFA stage with an authenticator or recovery code. The personal security screen exposes staff-only manual TOTP setup, one-time recovery-code display/replacement, accessible status/errors and a same-origin Admin return. Recovery codes cannot be replayed. Local proxying includes Admin/static paths only for this development return flow; deployed routing remains P13-02 evidence.
- Evidence: rebuilt the isolated dev dependency image with `django-allauth[headless,mfa]`, `fido2` and `qrcode`; pip check passed. Reviewed and applied upstream `mfa.0001`–`.0003` plus choices-only `tenancy.0008` only to `organicseoops-dev`, then restarted its four workers and Beat. Final `./backend-dev.sh test` passed99 PostgreSQL tests/system/current/drift; local `npm run check` passed32 Angular interaction tests, production build and3 static checks;9 deployment checks passed. Dev health returned200 and both nine-service stacks remained running. Security/Django reviews `ses_f2bb8bcb6ffeqYs8pUc3YxaWni` and `ses_f2bb8bc77ffeDQS2pC20vSwwHj` identified and closed the password-only privilege, plaintext-secret and lost-factor gaps. No production deployment/email, customer MFA, hosted CI/fresh scan or manual assistive-technology acceptance is claimed.

## D076: Address-Pinned Bounded Remote Fetch And Discovery

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-25, P07-01 local implementation.
- Scope boundary: A crawl scope contains either the project's exact canonical/alias hosts or one separately selected configured competitor host. Requests permit only HTTP/HTTPS on default ports without credentials/fragments. Resolve every request and redirect independently, require every answer to be globally routable, connect to the validated literal address, and verify TLS against the original hostname/SNI. Redirects remain inside the selected scope; external links cannot expand it.
- Resource and policy boundary: Use one monotonic deadline across timeout-killable isolated DNS, connect, nonblocking TLS handshake, request, headers and incremental body reads. Bound redirects, wire/decompressed bytes, encodings, sitemap response/recursion/count/URL output and recorded failures. Robots401/403/451 deny, only404/410 establish absence, and transient/other failures do not grant permission. Sitemap XML is bounded UTF-8 without declarations/entities; malformed/off-scope/limited discovery remains explicit.
- Evidence: twelve standard-library fixture tests include real loopback trickle-header/body deadlines, same-host DNS rebinding, mixed/private/metadata/alternate IPv4/IPv6 answers, TLS literal pin plus original hostname, redirects, compression overflow, robots states, malformed/unsafe XML and wide/looping/off-scope/deduplicated sitemap indexes. Security review `ses_f2915102bffeGPzoZOM6W7lpxg` found no remaining substantive issue after remediation. Full111 backend tests/system/current/drift,36 jobs tests and9 deployment checks passed; isolated resolver execution and dev health passed with both nine-service stacks running. No real remote request, crawler persistence/job, paid operation, deployment or legacy change occurred.

## D077: Marker-Gated Security Reviewer Invocation

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-25.
- Decision: Treat explicit **Security review gate** markers in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_TASKS.md` as the implementation allowlist for `security-reviewer`. `CONTINUE.md` and ordinary coding must not invoke it for unmarked cards. A marked card receives one bounded review after its source slice and focused checks; the main agent applies findings and verifies remediation without a routine repeat. Repeat only for an unresolved critical finding or remediation that materially redesigns the reviewed trust boundary, recording the reason in the checkpoint. A direct user request may invoke the reviewer independently of a marker.
- Execution: Use no interactive approval prompt, preserving unattended continuation. Keep `security-reviewer` on the existing `openai/gpt-5.6-sol`/`high` assignment for the smaller number of material gates, cap it at ten agentic iterations, and require supplied card/checkpoint/source/test paths before broader context. If an unmarked card unexpectedly creates a new trust boundary, synchronize a marker before delegation rather than silently widening invocation frequency.
- Rationale: Repeated Sol/high reviews within one card duplicate context loading, delay unattended implementation and add little value after straightforward remediation. A positive allowlist is safer and more maintainable than enumerating exclusions: future cards default to no review while the ledger visibly identifies the trust boundaries that require independent review.
- Validation: OpenCode1.18.32 `opencode agent list` discovered the reviewer and `opencode debug agent security-reviewer` resolved `openai/gpt-5.6-sol`/`high`, `steps: 10`, the marker-gate description/prompt, read/search/public-fetch access and denied edit/shell/delegation/environment access. Targeted documentation search confirmed D077 across `CONTINUE.md`, root guidance, plan, guide, ledger, checkpoint, TODO and shared reviewer resources; P07-02 is unmarked and P07-01's completed review is recorded as already satisfied. No live model review was invoked.

## D078: Immutable Resumable HTTP Crawl Observations

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-25, P07-02 local implementation.
- Observation boundary: Store one project/job-owned `CrawlRun` and append-only `CrawlPageSnapshot` per attempted requested URL. Preserve requested/final URL, redirects/status, bounded metadata/headings/directives/hreflang/links/JSON-LD candidates, validators, content hash, failure category and observation time. Raw HTML uses private project/job-scoped artifacts; conditional304 observations reference same-project prior content rather than fabricating a body. External links remain recorded evidence and never expand fetch scope.
- Execution boundary: Dispatch `crawl_site` only to the single-concurrency crawl queue with a1–25 page reservation, snapshotted one-second per-host delay, URL-family budget and270-second job deadline below Celery's300/330-second limits. Recheck current scope, cancellation, actor/project access and capacity before every request and redirect and after pacing. Fail closed on unavailable robots, preserve discovery/robots/page coverage failures, and distinguish useful partial results from failed/no-observation runs.
- Reliability boundary: A PostgreSQL session advisory lock permits only one live delivery per crawl job and releases on connection/process loss. Beat republishes bounded stale running crawls; redelivery reconstructs pending work from immutable snapshots. Usage settles from committed snapshots, not counters. Raw artifact keys are deterministic within a job and bounded terminal cleanup removes crash-staged unreferenced blobs while retaining referenced evidence. No browser execution, customer endpoint, schedule, competitor workflow or real remote crawl is activated by this decision.
- Evidence: reviewed `jobs.0007` applied only to `organicseoops-dev`; four workers and Beat restarted healthy. Thirty focused crawler/fetch/artifact tests cover project isolation, redirect-hop protections, host pacing, deadline, robots/sitemap coverage, hostile markup/charsets, conditional empty/nonempty reuse, cancellation/scope revocation, two-connection duplicate suppression, stale republish, durable settlement and changed-body crash cleanup. Jobs review `ses_f28f177d7ffehqeFYrghE5trTK` closed all source blockers. Final `./backend-dev.sh test` passed123 PostgreSQL tests/system/current/drift;9 deployment checks passed. Crawl task registration, crawl-only worker queue,300/330 limits, late ack/worker-loss rejection, dev health and both nine-service stacks were verified. No real remote request, customer/legacy data operation, provider call, deployment, hosted CI or fresh image scan occurred.

## D080: Immutable Coverage-Labeled Technical Diagnostics Foundation

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-25, bounded P07-04 local implementation; the card remains in progress pending an accepted project-specific parameter/pagination/facet rule contract.
- Evidence boundary: Add one immutable project/run-owned `TechnicalDiagnosticsSnapshot` whose hash binds terminal crawl/job inputs, project host scope, immutable page observations and latest rendered observations. Serialize creation against the job/project/run/page evidence and recheck cancellation and current actor access before persistence. Canonical diagnostics distinguish requested versus final URL evidence, conditional304 reuse, unchecked in-scope and unknown external targets, target redirects/directives/chains, neutral shared-target review candidates and complete versus partial raw/rendered disagreement. Existing protected crawl execution now queues observed in-scope canonical targets within the same page/family/deadline/robots budget and records sitemap URL provenance; diagnostics issue no independent fetch.
