Query analysis records bounded parameter names/shapes and pagination/high-count candidates without values or guessable value hashes. Bounded JSON-LD parser errors distinguish invalid syntax from capacity limits; type identifiers and visible `name`/`headline`/`description` checks remain dated observations. Rich-result eligibility and search appearance are explicitly not evaluated/unknown. Incomplete browser or truncated-text evidence cannot produce a definitive conflict or mismatch.

Preserve all meaningful variants and report `preserve_unconfigured`; do not invent blanket stripping, canonical/noindex recommendations or a generic rules engine. Analytics reconciliation and engine-reported indexing remain later source cards.

## D081: Explicit Project URL Parameter Rules

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-25, user selected the recommended P07-04 rule contract.
- Decision: Add project-owned exact case-sensitive parameter-name lists for ignored/tracking, pagination, and facet parameters plus one per-path parameterized-variant cap. Reject blank, malformed, duplicate or cross-category names. Unknown parameters are preserved by default; no built-in tracking list silently changes a customer's URLs.
- Crawl semantics: Remove configured ignored names only when deriving crawl identity, so tracking-only variants deduplicate without asserting canonical/noindex behavior. Preserve pagination, facet and unknown names and values as meaningful variants. Apply the configured cap to distinct parameterized crawl identities for one scheme/host/path while the existing total-page, URL-family, robots, pacing and deadline budgets remain authoritative. Record skipped parameter variants as partial coverage evidence.
- Diagnostic/UI semantics: Snapshot the accepted rule contract into each crawl job so later configuration changes cannot reinterpret historical evidence. Diagnostics report configured category matches and observed parameter shapes without values or value-derived hashes. Expose the fields in the existing private revision-guarded project onboarding/configuration resource; defaults are empty lists and a conservative cap of5. This decision adds no automatic canonical/noindex recommendation, public crawl endpoint or real remote operation.
- Evidence: `tenancy.0009` is current on isolated dev; new jobs use `crawl-http-v2`, while legacy v1 jobs without the policy retain their prior page-bound behavior. Exact-name/API, immutable snapshot, mixed-case identity, cap/coverage and diagnostic-hash fixtures pass without query values in output. Final132 backend PostgreSQL tests/system/current/drift,32 Angular tests/build/3 static checks,13 deployment/protocol checks and a rollback-only migrated-schema fixture passed. Bounded tenancy/jobs/Angular/SEO reviews closed actionable findings; no real remote/customer crawl, provider call, public endpoint, legacy operation or deployment occurred.

## D082: Exact-Origin HTTP-File Ownership Evidence

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-25, P12-01 local fixture-only implementation.
- Evidence contract: Support one initial HTTP-file method at `/.well-known/organicseoops-verification.txt` for an exact current project canonical origin or configured alias. Owner/admin issuance creates a high-entropy challenge, stores only its SHA-256 digest, returns plaintext only in that issuance response, and records pending/verified/revoked timestamps plus secret-free audit events. Project creation, competitor configuration and ordinary crawl access are not ownership evidence.
- Authorization lifecycle: A verified record is point-in-time evidence that remains effective only while its exact origin remains in current project scope and the record is not revoked; project origin/alias updates revoke displaced evidence. One reusable authorization function requires owner/admin permission, same organization/project, current origin and verified non-revoked evidence before future named passive work. P12-02 must bind and recheck that evidence, exact origin and an explicit observation allowlist; no automatic age expiry is selected yet.
- Execution boundary: Queue a rate-limited, zero-unit, digest-generation-bound `verify_project_origin` job only after locking and reloading its organization/project evidence. The single-concurrency audit queue uses D076 `CrawlScope` and `SafeFetcher` with a 10-second timeout, no redirects and a 256-byte body limit. Recheck actor permission, current scope, pending state, challenge generation and cancellation before requests and final persistence. PostgreSQL advisory locking suppresses duplicate execution; Beat republishes stale running work only after the same denial checks. No passive finding, arbitrary URL, competitor scope, exploit attempt, credential attack, port scan or real remote fetch is authorized.
- Evidence: Reviewed/applied `tenancy.0010` only to isolated dev and restarted workers/Beat/renderer healthy. Nine focused tests cover private API scope/secrecy, wrong domain/role/tenant, mismatch, success, cancellation, rotation, revocation, scope changes, throttling, audit-queue recovery and a two-connection queue/rotation race. Final `./backend-dev.sh test` passed 141 PostgreSQL tests plus system/current/drift; 13 deployment/protocol checks, Bash syntax, live task registration/audit-only queue inspection, migration currentness and dev health passed. Bounded tenancy/jobs reviews `ses_f2637cb2effedkbxGYwJnaKLtV` and `ses_f2637cb17ffegXhbs8I0aJE741` reported no blocking findings after remediation. No security-reviewer was invoked because P12-01 has no marked gate; P12-02 retains the combined required gate. No real remote/customer verification, passive observation, provider call, legacy operation, deployment or fresh vulnerability scan occurred.

## D093: English Root URLs And Turkish `/tr/` URLs

- Status: Accepted partial P3A-02 locale contract — 2026-09-27; resolves D092's public path/default choice.

Each published page is self-canonical and emits reciprocal `en`/`tr` hreflang only when the reviewed equivalent exists.

Missing Turkish pages remain absent rather than linking hreflang to placeholders or serving English under a Turkish URL.

## D094: Founder Publishing Ownership And Bilingual Human Review

- Status: Accepted partial P3A-02 content/review contract — 2026-09-27.

AI or machine translation may assist drafting but is not review evidence; revisions require the same approval when meaning, offer terms or material UI guidance changes.

Actual reviewer identity/availability, reviewed translations, support staffing and release evidence remain required before P3A-02 completion.

## D095: Thirty-Day No-Card Organization Trial And Turkey Seller Jurisdiction

- Status: Accepted partial P3A-04 commercial policy — 2026-09-27.
- Decision: Grant a 30-day trial at most once to each eligible organization after verified-user organization creation. Trial activation requires no payment card. At expiry, the organization enters the planned read-only/retained-results state; paid access starts only after an owner deliberately completes hosted checkout. Invitations, callbacks, projects, email changes and ownership changes never create, extend or reset a trial.
- Seller context: The business selling OrganicSEOOps will be legally established in Turkey. This selects seller jurisdiction, not a customer market, billing currency, tax treatment or billing provider. Those require provider acceptance and appropriate legal/accounting review.
- Dated provider constraint: Stripe's official global-availability page did not list Turkey as a supported account country on 2026-09-27. Paddle's official material says it supports software suppliers except its stated unsupported-country list, which does not include Turkey, and supports TRY payment presentation; Lemon Squeezy's official material lists Turkey for bank payouts and TRY selling presentation but processes transactions and payouts in USD. Paddle and Lemon Squeezy both describe merchant-of-record operation. This narrows research but does not establish account approval or select a provider.
- Remaining policy: Define repeat-organization abuse controls, paid auto-renewal/cancellation disclosure, provider and stable API, base/presentment/payout currencies, plans/prices/allowances, proration/downgrade/grace/refund rules and retained export/deletion/backup periods. No trial, checkout, provider account, payment acceptance or public offer is activated by this decision.

## D096: Paddle Billing, USD Base Pricing And Monthly Renewal

- Status: Accepted partial P3A-04 provider/lifecycle policy — 2026-09-27; narrows D095's remaining commercial choices.
- Provider: Target Paddle Billing as merchant of record, subject to Turkey seller-account approval, actual terms and sandbox/live acceptance. Use the current Paddle Billing REST API version `1`, sent explicitly as `Paddle-Version: 1`; do not use Paddle Classic contracts. Backend credentials remain server-only, Paddle.js receives only its environment-specific publishable client token, and sandbox/live identifiers never mix.
- Currency: Define catalog/base prices in USD and use Paddle's official localized price preview/checkout to present TRY to Turkish buyers where supported. Provider-returned currency, tax and totals are authoritative for checkout display; never calculate or hard-code exchange rates in OrganicSEOOps. Payout currency/account and customer markets still require approval/accounting confirmation.
- Eligibility control: Automatically grant the D095 trial only to a verified user's first eligible organization. A later organization created by the same user requires an audited staff eligibility approval before its one lifetime trial; invitations and ownership changes do not create another trial. Do not add device fingerprinting or treat this rule as complete fraud detection.
- Renewal/cancellation: After deliberate owner checkout, launch with monthly automatic renewal only. Checkout and pricing must disclose recurrence, amount/currency/tax treatment and cancellation behavior. Customers can cancel at any time through a fresh, temporary Paddle customer-portal session; cancellation is effective at period end and paid access remains through that period. A fully canceled subscription requires a new checkout to return.
- Verified provider boundary: Official Paddle documentation checked 2026-09-27 states API version `1`, separate sandbox/live base URLs and keys, temporary non-cacheable portal-session links, period-end portal cancellation, localized pricing/TRY support, signed webhook verification and webhook simulation. This is documentation evidence only, not account approval or a passed integration.
- Remaining policy: Decide offer shape and exact prices/allowances, early paid conversion timing, payout currency, customer markets, proration/downgrade, payment-failure grace, refunds/chargebacks and retained results/export/deletion/backup periods. No Paddle account, catalog, credential, dependency, checkout or payment is created by this decision.

## D097: One Agency Plan, Bounded Allowances And Immediate Early Conversion

- Status: Accepted partial P3A-04 offer policy — 2026-09-27; records planned offer version `agency-launch-v1` but does not activate or advertise it.
- Offer: Launch with one self-serve monthly Agency plan at a USD 149 base price per organization before provider-calculated tax. Paddle's returned localized TRY price, currency, tax and total remain authoritative where available. There is no launch annual plan or second self-serve tier; contact/support for needs beyond the plan is not an unpublished custom entitlement.
- Allowances: The 30-day trial permits one active project, three organization members and 40 internal units across the trial. The paid plan permits three active projects, ten organization members and 100 internal units per monthly paid entitlement period. Internal units are bounded operational request/page work, not money, provider cost or purchasable credits. Current internal safety ceilings remain independent controls; final affordability and advertised feature/source coverage require P3A-12 evidence before offer activation.
- Early conversion: If an owner deliberately completes checkout during an active trial, Paddle charges immediately, the trial ends immediately and the monthly paid entitlement period starts immediately. The checkout UI must disclose this consequence before confirmation; unused trial time or units do not delay charging or extend the first paid period.
- Boundary: Features, integrations, schedules, overages and provider-funded work are included only when their separate readiness and budget gates pass. Changing the accepted price or allowance requires a versioned replacement decision rather than silently editing an active offer.
- Remaining policy: Confirm Paddle seller approval/sandbox, payout currency/account and supported customer markets; decide payment-failure grace and recovery, refunds/chargebacks, tax/accounting treatment and retained results/export/deletion/backup periods. With one launch plan, plan-to-plan upgrade/downgrade and proration are not launch flows. No Paddle account, product/price catalog, credential, checkout, payment, billing code or public offer is created by this decision.

## D098: Global Buyer Launch, USD Payout And Retained-Access Lifecycle

- Status: Accepted P3A-04 market/payment-failure/refund/retention policy — 2026-09-27; public activation remains blocked on the evidence below.
- Customer market and support: Permit self-serve launch to business and consumer buyers globally where Paddle lawfully supports the transaction and OrganicSEOOps can meet applicable requirements. Product content, critical email and support remain English/Turkish only; do not imply localization, support, payment methods or availability in every territory. Paddle checkout's accepted country, currency, tax and total are authoritative, while global consumer-law/privacy/terms review remains a launch gate.
- Payout: Intend USD monthly wire payout to an approved Turkish business bank account, matching the USD balance/base price. Paddle and the receiving bank must approve the account; Turkish accounting must confirm payout/invoice/tax treatment and actual bank/SWIFT fees. Do not advertise or configure TRY payout: Paddle's current payout list does not include TRY.
- Failed payment: A verified `past_due` renewal keeps paid service for seven calendar days from the provider failure time, subject to normal limits. After seven days, stop new costly work, pause schedules, stop running work at safe boundaries and retain only read-only results, export and payment recovery. A verified successful recovery restores paid service without replaying accumulated schedules or resetting consumed usage; provider cancellation after exhausted recovery starts the lapsed-access retention clock.
- Refunds and chargebacks: Follow Paddle's then-current buyer terms/refund process and mandatory law. Period-end cancellation has no automatic prorated refund. Paddle handles statutory withdrawal, duplicate/non-delivery/defect and discretionary requests; a confirmed full refund ends paid service. A verified chargeback/dispute immediately stops costly work and leaves read-only/export access while reviewed, restoring or ending service only from reconciled provider truth.
- Retention: Trial expiry, effective paid cancellation and payment-recovery cancellation receive 90 days of read-only retained results, authorized export and checkout/recovery access. Then delete customer content from active systems within 30 days. Deliberate owner organization closure disables access and work immediately and deletes customer content from active systems within 30 days. Customer-bearing backups expire through a maximum 35-day rotation after active-system deletion; do not promise immediate backup erasure. Billing, fraud, security, audit and legal records may remain separately restricted for counsel-approved periods and must not preserve general customer content by default.
- Verified provider boundary: Official Paddle material checked 2026-09-27 lists USD but not TRY among payout currencies, describes monthly wire/Payoneer payouts and a possible conversion margin when balance and payout currencies differ, represents service for Turkish sellers/global sales, maps failed renewals to `past_due` then recovered `active` or exhausted `canceled`, and documents a default 30-day Retain dunning window. Paddle's refund policy dated 2026-03-31 states mandatory local rights, including a 14-day Turkish-consumer withdrawal right, otherwise generally non-refundable transactions plus defect/discretionary handling and possible suspension during chargebacks. This evidence does not establish seller approval, legal advice or successful sandbox/live behavior.
- Remaining gate: Obtain Paddle seller/domain/bank approval, confirm the configured dunning and webhook behavior in sandbox, complete global terms/privacy/consumer-law and Turkish accounting review, validate P3A-12 costs/source coverage, and verify P13-04 deletion/restore behavior. No account, payout setting, customer transaction, refund, deletion or provider-side operation is authorized or performed by this decision.

## D099: One Bounded Seven-Day Trial Extension

- Status: Accepted P3A-05 support policy — 2026-09-27; selected under the user's delegation of non-credential product decisions.
- Decision: Permit at most one seven-calendar-day extension for an `agency-launch-v1` organization trial. The maximum trial end is the original start plus 37 days; the extension does not reset the start, usage or allowances and cannot be granted once that extended end has passed.
- Authorization: Only active platform staff with the trial-change permission through the existing MFA-protected platform path may extend a trial. Require a bounded reason and immutable before/after audit. Lock the organization and trial so concurrent or repeated requests cannot extend twice.
- Eligibility: Permit an active trial or an expired/read-only trial whose day-37 deadline is still in the future. Reject pending-review, suspended, policy-drifted or already-extended trials and suspended organizations. Restore an explicitly expired/read-only trial to active for its remaining extended time; server time continues to derive expiry.
- Boundary: This is a support exception, not a customer entitlement or recurring retention mechanism. It does not increase limits or erase usage. A future paid-entitlement model must prevent trial extension after paid conversion and synchronize any allowed correction with provider truth before public billing activation.

## D100: Finish Credential-Free Coding Before Requesting Access

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-27; user clarified the default instruction “continue coding by following CONTINUE.md.”
- Decision: On an unavailable Google Search Console, other provider, billing or deployment credential/account/approval/quota/budget/environment, record the exact blocked card and required acceptance, then continue every dependency-ready authorized credential-free card or safely separable local slice across phases. Once no such work remains within the requested scope, summarize remaining blocked cards and required access for the user. An explicitly narrower user task may require an earlier clarification.

Credentials stay in approved private configuration, never in chat, committed files or handoff records. This refines D068's continuation order without authorizing deployment, paid operations or new account access.

## D101: Positive-Unit Trial Enforcement And Zero-Unit Read-Only Utilities

- Status: Accepted P3A-08a local enforcement policy — 2026-09-27; selected under the user's delegation of non-credential product decisions.
- Costly-work boundary: A job that reserves one or more internal units requires an active, unexpired, exact-policy `agency-launch-v1` trial until paid entitlements exist. Missing historical trial state fails closed. Pending, expired/read-only, suspended or policy-drifted trial state cannot start or continue positive-unit work.
- Trial allowance: Enforce the accepted 40-unit trial allowance across the trial's half-open UTC window using settled actual units plus currently reserved estimates and excluding released reservations. Reservation occurs atomically under the organization lock so concurrent requests cannot exceed the allowance. Trial extension widens the same window but does not reset consumption.
- Zero-unit boundary: Diagnostic probes and ownership verification may remain available in trial read-only state because they consume no commercial units. Existing authentication, role, organization suspension, reservation integrity and internal concurrency controls still apply; this does not make them public launch features or retained customer-content exports.
- Denial behavior: Preserve distinct `trial_unavailable`, `trial_units_exhausted`, `internal_capacity` and `usage_unreserved` reasons across reservation, dispatch and costly-stage rechecks. Pause schedules on commercial denial; internal-capacity deferral remains separate. Paid composition, project/member limits, production backfill and public activation remain blocked on their owning cards.

## D102: Versioned Acquisition Milestones From Authoritative Records

- Status: Accepted partial P3A-11 measurement contract — 2026-09-27; no instrumentation or public funnel is activated.
- Dictionary: Use plan §29.2's `acquisition-v1` IDs and grains. Current records provide the identity/current state for registration and verified email, but historical self-service origin and verification time are unavailable and must be captured prospectively at their committed transitions. Eligible organization trial activation and first complete nonempty CSV import are derivable from current operational records. Trial conversion is organization-grained; invited teammates, callback retries, extensions and repeated imports cannot create another trial/first-import conversion.
- Authority and privacy: Use the dictionary's server-side deduplication keys from internal authoritative primary keys without exporting those keys as event properties or claiming deduplication is implemented. Allow only the direct-identifier-free, data-minimized properties listed in the dictionary. Route, CTA, locale, consent and time can still be pseudonymous behavioral data. Never include names, emails, organization/project names, domains, URLs/query strings, property/file identifiers, tokens, customer SEO data or free text. Operational records remain authoritative; no separate analytics copy or retention period is selected.
- Unavailable milestones: Public views/CTAs, first successful customer audit, first accepted action, checkout initiation and confirmed paid activation remain planned until their routes, consent-aware collector and owning records exist. A checkout return never proves payment; paid activation requires reconciled backend/provider state. Current diagnostic/crawl/performance jobs do not silently become the accepted first-value audit.
- Remaining work: Select analytics/consent tooling, event emission, analytics retention/deletion, denominators, attribution/cohort windows and first useful result; validate public/app/email/Paddle handoffs and deduplication at P3A-11/P13-05. Unknown linkage remains explicit and does not justify fingerprinting.

## D103: Build Configurable Product Before Live Provider Or Deployment Validation

- Status: Accepted user correction — 2026-09-27; supersedes D100's credential-request stopping rule, D083's deferral of customer-entered provider credentials and any earlier task dependency that requires a representative live call, acquired domain or actual deployment **before implementation**. Historical local/fixture-only evidence remains local/fixture-only.
- Project examples: `transfertakip.com` and `airporttransferhub.com` are examples, not seed data, mandatory initial projects, authorized crawl targets or required Google properties. Users create organizations/projects and select their own domain, source property and integrations through the UI. The supplied Search Console screenshot showing a domain property is context, not permission to ingest data or a reason to require a new URL-prefix property for coding. Support verified domain-wide properties only after a bounded project-scope contract prevents cross-project data mixing; never treat domain-wide aggregate rows as exact-origin observations without supported filtering.
- Implementation target: Continue dependency-ready application, billing, provider, frontend and deployment-preparation cards without asking this user for any Google/Paddle/AI keys, project ownership, provider budget, domain acquisition, model selection or AWS/GitHub access. Build real owner/admin and MFA-protected staff UI flows, provider capability-aware model selection, guarded jobs, secure credential entry/rotation/disconnect, provider adapters, checkout/portal/webhook/reconciliation and deployment files/runbooks; verify with deterministic fixtures, local emulators or permitted synthetic checks. An unavailable live account is not a coding stop or a reason to mark a feature impossible. Do not invent success or pre-populate real domains/accounts.
- Credential boundary: Platform-operator credentials (Paddle seller API/webhook secret, Google OAuth application credentials, platform-funded AI/performance keys) are entered through protected MFA-backed staff settings, write-only after submission and stored encrypted with rotation; IAM/bootstrap encryption keys remain private infrastructure configuration rather than customer UI inputs. Organization owner/admin may enter permitted own-provider AI/performance keys through scoped write-only UI where BYOK is supported; provider-specific OAuth grants use the browser consent/connection UI, not pasted refresh tokens. User-facing project/job model selection is separate from credential ownership. Never expose secrets in responses, Admin, logs, frontend builds or task arguments, and do not weaken tenant, rate, cost or revocation controls.
- Evidence split: A card may pass **implementation acceptance** with runnable local contract/security/tenant/race/UI checks and marked security review, while live provider/sandbox representative checks and actual hosted deployment remain **release-readiness evidence** on P3A-12/P3A-13/P13-05 and relevant capability-specific gates. Record unavailable provider operations honestly; do not call a mock a live integration, charge a card or advertise unsupported access. Deployment work means checked IaC/workflows/templates/runbooks and safe local route/restore simulation; actual AWS/GitHub deployment and production migrations require a separate explicit request. Git initialization and commits remain separately requested operations.
- Delivery: Do not request credentials in chat or steer users to `backend/.env` to complete application code. Give later operator/customer setup instructions through the finished UI and deployment runbooks. Continue sequentially under `CONTINUE.md` until authorized coding work, not provider access, is exhausted. Public offer, real integration claims and production release still require account approval, licensed/actual access, terms/cost and end-to-end evidence at their release gates.
- Publication boundary: D094's founder/bilingual review remains required before publishing locale copy, not before building the preference, i18n routing and protected draft pipeline. Drafts must not be presented as translated, approved or indexable production pages.

## D104: SES Transactional Email With Private Feedback Queue

- Status: Accepted P3A-10 technical implementation choice — 2026-09-28; local contract work only, no sender account/domain or AWS operation. D105 records the host-IAM isolation correction found during its marked review.
- Sender: Use Django's authenticated STARTTLS SMTP against a configured Amazon SES relay, with API and default notification worker as the only mail-capable processes. Keep SMTP credentials private infrastructure configuration; no frontend/Beat/AI/crawl/audit access. Verification/reset links and customer-specific mail remain protected from logs, task arguments and public assets. A queued token-free notice is not confirmation of delivery.
- Feedback: Subscribe only the intended SES feedback SNS topic to a private SQS queue; a policy restricts SES publication to the exact identity/account and another restricts queue publication to that topic. Use a dedicated EC2 worker host/role for queue receive/delete; Docker Compose environment omission is not per-container IAM isolation on a shared host. The normal app host must not have queue IAM rights, and the worker host needs separately checked private DB/Redis access (P13). Read through the AWS SDK/IAM path rather than accepting unauthenticated HTTPS callbacks or fetching SNS-supplied certificate URLs. Validate the exact topic, region/account, sender, event kind/date and recipient scope from bounded JSON; deduplicate feedback for any exact stored EmailAddress and suppress current verified-address mail on permanent bounce or complaint. Transient bounces and ambiguous multi-recipient complaints remain explicitly unconfirmed; service and recovery flows must not silently claim delivery. Manual staff review of uncertain SMTP outcomes may resend once with acknowledged duplicate risk.
- Gates: D103 permits local fixtures without SES/SNS/SQS access. Real sender/domain approval, SPF/DKIM/DMARC, queue policy/IAM, SES sandbox/production feedback, reviewed English/Turkish critical copy, notification timing, suppression recovery and cost remain release checks. P3A-10's new incoming-feedback trust boundary has one explicit D077 Security review gate after focused checks. This technical selection does not authorize a live email/provider/AWS request or weaken project/recipient authorization.

## D105: Dedicated Feedback Worker Identity On EC2

- Status: Accepted P3A-10 marked-review remediation — 2026-09-28; local topology templates only, actual AWS identity/network checks unrun.
- Correction: D104's original worker-only *environment mapping* on a shared EC2 Compose host was insufficient: all outbound containers could acquire the same instance-profile credentials through IMDS. Place the SES-feedback-reading `worker-default` on a separate EC2 host/role that runs only this service with private authenticated DB/Redis and artifact access. The ordinary API/Beat/other-worker host must not have queue receive/delete IAM permissions. Move feedback queue/topic environment to a worker-host-only Compose override; never treat omitted variables as container IAM isolation. Preserve the single default queue and existing image separation; P13-01/P13-03 own the actual private inter-host/runtime/IAM and release/rollback templates.
- Publisher and recovery: The SES identity/account is the only SNS publisher, SNS topic is the only SQS sender, and an encrypted source/DLQ stack must redrive malformed messages after five receives. Record permanent feedback against an exact stored email address even before verification/primary transition, but suppress sending and permit staff clearance only for the current verified address. Production SES SMTP host must match its configured AWS region. These are local contracts; real SES delivery, IAM denial from every non-feedback workload, source/DLQ policy and approved sender/domain remain P3A-13/P13-05 release checks.

- Local implementation status clarification2026-10-09: P13-01/P13-03 already accept D105's split-host/private-network/IAM and release/rollback template contracts. P3A-10's earlier local split-host blocker is superseded by that evidence, without changing D105's policy or reinterpreting fixtures as actual hosted isolation/delivery. Reviewed bilingual critical copy, sender/domain/SES feedback and hosted negative-role/network evidence remain gates.

## D106: Same-Origin Browser API Through The Static Host

- Status: Accepted P13-01 implementation topology — 2026-09-28; D107 corrects its origin Host forwarding detail after tracing exact OAuth callbacks. Actual DNS/TLS/CloudFront/API behavior unrun.
- Decision: Retain Angular's single static `organicseoops.com` browser host and existing relative `/api` and `/_allauth` calls with `credentials: same-origin` and host-only session/CSRF cookies. CloudFront must route only explicitly declared dynamic paths (including account/OAuth callbacks) to the separate HTTPS Django origin at the intended `api.organicseoops.com`, with caching disabled and cookies, method, Origin, Host/proxy headers and CSRF handled deliberately. Return paths, OAuth state and login/reset fragments remain on the public root host; signed provider webhooks can use the API host directly. Do not proxy unknown public routes or missing assets to Django and do not use a blanket SPA error rewrite.
- Rationale: Direct cross-origin API calls with the current browser code would omit cookies, and a host-only CSRF cookie from the API host is inaccessible to scripts on the public host. A same-origin CDN path proxy preserves the existing account flows while allowing a separate Django origin/container deployment; no broad `Domain=.organicseoops.com` session cookie or cross-origin token storage is introduced.
- Gate: Acquire/deploy the actual hosts and certificates, verify CloudFront cache/origin-request policies, CORS/CSRF/cookie/OAuth callback and signed webhook behavior against those hosts, and test unknown public/missing asset HTTP status at P13-01–P13-05. Route configuration files and local fixtures do not establish deployed auth or production readiness.

## D107: Preserve The Public Browser Host At Django OAuth Callbacks

- Status: Accepted P13-01 correction — 2026-09-28; local static/API contract only, no live domain or OAuth operation.
- Replaces D106's origin Host detail: The Search Console and Analytics OAuth callbacks compare the configured absolute redirect URI against Django's `request.build_absolute_uri(request.path)` and bind state to that exact URL. CloudFront forwarding **only** the API origin Host would produce `api.organicseoops.com`, while the browser session/host-only cookie and registered redirect URI live at `organicseoops.com`. Preserve the public viewer `Host` on only the explicit `/api/*` and `/_allauth/*` HTTPS origin behaviors (CloudFront managed AllViewer policy), with CloudFront aliases and Django `ALLOWED_HOSTS` limited to the accepted public/API hosts. Keep all browser session/CSRF/OAuth links rooted at the public host; direct API-host webhooks require signed-provider validation and do not widen session cookie scope.
- TLS requirement: The separate API HTTPS origin certificate must cover the public viewer host **and** API origin name so CloudFront can validate the forwarded Host/SNI and direct origin hostname; server must accept the exact public Host at the proxy/Django boundary. Do not trust arbitrary `X-Forwarded-Host` from clients or use parent-domain cookies as a workaround.
- Checks/gate: Local tests cover public-Host OAuth absolute callback equality, origin/cookie/CSRF acceptance and foreign Host denial; actual CloudFront→origin TLS/Host/ALLOWED_HOSTS, registered Google redirect, browser cookie and direct signed-webhook behavior remain P13-01/P13-05 hosted release evidence. This correction does not authorize domain acquisition, provider consent or deployment.

## D108: Restrict The HTTPS API Origin To CloudFront And Forward Verified Viewer Identity

- Status: Accepted P13-01 API-origin preparation — 2026-09-28; source/NGINX syntax checks only, no ALB/domain/provider deployment.
- Origin boundary: Put the EC2 Django proxy behind an HTTPS ALB in public subnets whose security group allows only the AWS-managed CloudFront origin-facing prefix list on443. Its target group reaches the EC2 proxy on a private VPC port80 restricted to the ALB security group. Keep ALB health `/api/health/` distinct from browser routes. CloudFront's explicit same-origin dynamic paths preserve the public `Host` per D107 and forward trusted CloudFront-generated `CloudFront-Viewer-Address` for per-client throttling; the production proxy rejects ordinary paths when that header is absent, derives a single viewer IP for Django and sets trusted HTTPS forwarding. Only the ALB security group may reach the proxy; host/Origin/CSRF still require backend validation.
- Staff and providers: Route MFA-protected Django Admin and WhiteNoise Admin assets on explicit root-host `/admin/*` and `/static/*` CloudFront behaviors rather than exposing an unrelated direct API-host cookie/login flow. Register signed Paddle webhooks on the canonical public-root `/api/*` path through CloudFront and verify raw signatures there. The API hostname names the HTTPS origin but is not a public bypass of the CloudFront/ALB security-group boundary. Static customer landing/app requests stay on the private S3/OAC origin; no blanket SPA fallback is added.
- Gates: The ALB certificate must cover both public viewer Host and API origin name; actual CloudFront-generated headers, IPv4/IPv6 client address behavior, ALB/SG/Host forwarding, OAuth/MFA/browser cookies, signature byte identity and request throttling need an authorized deployed test. Template/source/syntax fixtures are not that test. This narrows D106/D107's optional direct API-host webhook path in favor of one canonical browser/webhook ingress.

- Source/roles: Keep initial public bodies and metadata in the repository's Angular semantic components and route inventory, rather than introducing a marketing CMS before there is a real non-developer editing need. The founder is editor and publishing owner; an identified bilingual human reviews each actual English/Turkish page revision and applicable policy/offer/proof before it can be published. Maintain review history and a prior compatible static build/HTML for controlled rollback; repeat human review when claims, prices, sources, copy or translations change.

Local hashes or fixtures never attest human review or make the product offer live.

It must label absent anonymous/audit/action/checkout/paid stages unavailable, report no rates/attribution, and export no customer identifiers. Consent, lifetime retention, anonymized grain, true checkout/paid handoff and first value remain P3A-11/P3A-13 release work.

## D112: Preserve Search And Crawl Outcomes While Conversion Source Is Unavailable

## D114: Universal Shared Roles And Sol 6.1 Subagent Routing

- Status: Accepted — 2026-09-30, explicit user request for portable subagents and the Sol upgrade.
- Decision: Keep the eight authoritative reviewer contracts in `.agents/roles/` and register the same roster through thin OpenCode, Codex, and Claude adapters. Every adapter explicitly loads the shared role; role behavior is never duplicated in host files.

New Claude adapters inherit the invoking Claude model rather than using an incompatible OpenAI identifier. Existing primary-model defaults and built-in exploration routing are outside this subagent change.

Claude permits only Read/Grep/Glob/WebFetch, with ten turns for security. Codex uses a read-only sandbox and explicit no-command/no-operational-API/no-secret/no-delegation instructions; it has no equivalent per-agent turn-limit field here. Host runtime overrides still apply, so syntax validation is not proof of identical sandbox enforcement.
- Replaces: D046's six current Sol version pins and D035/D045's OpenCode-only native registration; D047's Angular routing and D077's invocation policy remain.
- References: [Codex custom agents](https://developers.openai.com/codex/subagents), [Claude subagents](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents), and [OpenCode agents](https://opencode.ai/docs/agents/).
- Validation: All 24 OrganicSEOOps TOML/YAML adapters and eight shared-role registrations per host passed static checks. OpenCode 1.18.33 resolved all eight reviewers with expected models/reasoning and denied edit/bash/task tools; security retained ten steps and its marker-gated prompt. Catalog evidence confirms GPT-6.1 Sol/high. Codex 0.147.0 is installed but no spawned-session test was run; Claude Code is absent, so its checks were static only. No live reviewer/model call occurred.

## D115: Universal Maintenance For All Agent Resources

## D117: Bounded GDELT News Metadata Discovery

- Status: Accepted local P09-02 implementation choice — 2026-10-01, within authorized configurable source implementation; no live provider request or availability claim.
- Provider: Select GDELT DOC 2.0 ArticleList RSS as the initial news source. Its official terms allow commercial use/redistribution of GDELT datasets with attribution and a link; that does not license publisher article bodies or images. Store only bounded provider-returned title/link/date metadata, retain GDELT attribution, and never fetch publisher content as part of discovery. Actual representative response/terms/coverage readiness remains P3A-12.
- Selection: One explicitly selected current project seed phrase and one currently allowed, nonblocked HTTPS publisher origin, last one or seven days, at most twenty records. Construct a fixed official API request with an escaped exact phrase and generated exact domain filter; accept no arbitrary endpoint/query operators. The provider search operates on English/translingual matching; project market/language are human context, not verified source-country/language attribution. Returned article links must independently pass exact publisher allow/block scope and ordinary safe research selection before later fetch.
- Budget/reliability: Reuse the explicit discovery job, private project snapshots, current actor/configuration/cancel/expiry checks and one internal request unit. Protected fixed-host transport has bounded bytes/deadline and no redirects. Top-N metadata is incomplete coverage, not popularity, search volume, competition or evidence of no demand; failed optional news must preserve independent successful sources. No new credential store, provider SDK, generic connector or automatic schedule is needed for this source.
- Optional sources: Current official YouTube search contract is available but requires its own configured key, quota/retention and applicable-project selection; no video connector is claimed from news code. Official Trends remains capability-gated until its actual documented supported contract is available; no unofficial scraper or alpha endpoint is invented. P09-02 remains unfinished until its locally applicable source/UI/check work passes.
- Documentation checked: [GDELT DOC 2.0](https://blog.gdeltproject.org/gdelt-doc-2-0-api-debuts/) and [terms](https://www.gdeltproject.org/about.html#termsofuse), [YouTube search.list](https://developers.google.com/youtube/v3/docs/search/list), [official Trends announcement](https://developers.google.com/search/blog/2025/07/trends-api), 2026-10-01. These are public documentation reads only, not runtime/capability verification.
- Current Trends follow-up: [Official alpha landing page](https://developers.google.com/search/apis/trends), checked 2026-10-01, still invites a limited set of testers and supplies no public endpoint/response schema; the announced five-year scaled-interest capability is not a locally usable contract. Keep it unavailable until that contract can be implemented rather than requesting a user credential or inventing an API.

## D118: Separate YouTube Search Metadata From Topic Scores

- Status: Accepted P09-02 local source-contract implementation choice — 2026-10-01; no live API, key or provider approval.
- Current official constraints: YouTube developer policies III.C.5 prohibit altering/replacing search results or intermixing other sources as YouTube results; III.E.4 limits temporary public API data to at most thirty days before refresh/deletion and prohibits API-derived data/metrics; III.D.1 requires credentials for the API Client's own single API project, not quota circumvention through other projects. Public search needs no private-user OAuth scopes. Follow the currently documented separate Search Queries bucket (search.list documentation says one unit/call with100 default daily calls), not the historical100-unit-per-call assumption; actual quota remains an operator-configured release check.
- Implementation: Add a separate bounded public-video search view retaining requested/returned scope, provider order and unchanged requested metadata. Do not put YouTube responses into generic scored TopicCandidateSnapshot/TopicReview, AI synthesis or durable content/export copies. Do not infer view counts, popularity, trends or business value from video search. No audiovisual/thumbnail download, embed/autoplay, social action or private-channel access.
- Credential/retention target: A dedicated write-only MFA-protected platform YouTube key and one client-wide budget, separate from AI/performance/OAuth credentials; supported organization BYOK is not established by this provider policy and must not circumvent quota. User source selection comes from current project configuration. Keep public metadata in a separately expiring record (initial seven-day maximum, stricter than the documented thirty-day ceiling) with read-time expiry, explicit delete, bounded purge/backup/export treatment before enabling it. No background refresh or automatic paid work. Customer first-party channel analytics/OAuth belongs to its own later requested capability.
- Disclosure: Dedicated YouTube attribution/Terms, Google privacy and feature-specific draft data-use/retention information plus explicit user acceptance precede a locally tested search. ## D119: Response-Only YouTube Search Delivery

- Status: Accepted bounded P09-02 implementation choice — 2026-10-01, within existing local authorization; policies/search documentation rechecked, no operational request.
- Replaces D118's proposed seven-day persisted metadata record with a simpler response-only consumer. One explicit bounded search (ten-second transport, first twenty results only) returns unchanged requested API metadata in a private no-store response. Browser component memory expires after fifteen minutes and clears on route/scope changes, page hiding, reload, another search or explicit clear. No browser storage, media/embed, download, scoring, AI handoff or server-side response cache.
- Lifecycle: No returned API metadata, video/channel IDs, returned region or paging token is written to PostgreSQL, Redis, artifacts, logs, reports, exports or backups. Existing 35-day database backups would outlive the provider's 30-day public-data limit; avoiding stored API data removes that conflict without changing customer retention. Persist only user-selected inputs/policy-version acceptance, categorical attempt status and operational internal-unit accounting in existing scoped JobRun/UsageReservation records. These are not API-derived metrics. Full customer export/identity blockers remain unchanged.
- Credential/quota: Dedicated encrypted singleton platform YouTube key, MFA-protected write-only staff UI and fixed immutable API project identity; no environment/performance/AI/OAuth fallback or customer BYOK. Reuse infrastructure provider encryption keys, never another provider credential. Staff configure a conservative 1–100 daily Search Queries request ceiling shared across all tenants, resetting at America/Los_Angeles midnight. Rotation/revocation cannot reset usage or change the API project. Accepted attempts count conservatively even if a request fails before reaching Google; one client-wide in-flight attempt, no automatic retry or paging. Existing organization/project commercial/internal limits also apply.
- Delivery: Recheck actor/current project configuration, key revision, cancellation and entitlement before secret delivery and before response. Duplicate/uncertain requests never replay; an interrupted request becomes unavailable, its consumed reservation settles without a second provider request. This bounded synchronous response is a feature-specific exception to the long-running Celery path, required to avoid persisted response transport/cache; all longer work remains on existing workers.
- Feature-specific draft Terms/privacy and YouTube/Google links are visible before explicit per-request versioned acceptance. The UI states top-N/incomplete coverage, region viewability and relevance-language preference limits; no market demand claim. Actual provider, legal/privacy/bilingual and hosted response/log/backup verification remain P3A-12/P3A-13/P13-05 release evidence. Add P09-02's explicit Security review gate for this new credential and ephemeral-delivery boundary before its one post-focused-check review.

## D120: Initial API-Text Visibility Prompt Methodology

- Status: Accepted local P11A-01 implementation contract — 2026-10-01, within bounded AI-visibility scope. No provider request, model default or consumer-chat availability claim.

- Methodology: Owner/admin-reviewed project/market/language prompt sets, at most five exact human-authored500-character prompts, separated into neutral discovery and branded groups. Every prompt references a same-project exact-current intent review; retain explicit human coverage rationale and corrections as immutable revisions. Reuse the existing alias-boundary classifier with current brand aliases/configured domains as a conservative lexical guard (neutral excludes, branded includes); this does not certify semantic neutrality, completeness or genuine customer demand. Prompts are submitted unchanged by a future collector; project brand/audience is never invisibly injected into neutral prompts. Stored requested market/language are human context, not asserted applied provider controls.

One internal unit per attempted request, existing commercial/internal concurrency gates, no automatic fallback/retry or background research. Repeat policy is user-triggered, at least24hours per set/prompt; automatic schedules remain unavailable until their own checked collector. P11A-01 records this policy; P11A-02 owns its actual dispatch/attempt/cooldown enforcement and usage/unknown recovery. Saving prompts has no provider cost and cannot start work.
- Data handling: Prompt methodology and future answer evidence are private customer data governed by D098's active/retained-access/deletion/35-day backup lifecycle, scoped ownership/export/delete registry and untrusted-text rendering. The existing adapter uses `store:false`, but this is not Zero Data Retention: current official data-control documentation describes default abuse-monitoring retention up to30days with legal/safety exceptions and possible up-to24hour prompt-cache application state. No ZDR or geographic-residency promise. User prompts must avoid secrets/personal/customer-export payloads; provider/counsel review remains release evidence.
- Sources: [OpenAI API data controls](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/your-data), read2026-10-01; existing supported Responses transport D084/store:false inspected. The official [Services Agreement](https://openai.com/policies/services-agreement/) fetch returned403; current account-specific terms/licensing approval is explicitly unverified and must be checked before live sampling/public claims. P11A-01 methodology can be fixture-checked now; collection remains a separate unfinished card, not a consumer automation workaround.
- Execution reconciliation2026-10-01: P11A-02 implements this initial manual collector; P11A-02s explicitly retains the older scheduled-repeat and additional licensed/manual-observation scope behind its own reviewed methodology/access/retention/budget choice. This clarifies D120 precedence without dropping the long-term scheduling requirement or claiming consumer observation support. Current official data controls rechecked; no actual provider operation.

## D122: Data-Minimized Customer Bot-Log JSONL Imports

- Status: Accepted bounded P11-06 local format/privacy contract — 2026-10-01; no actual log, upload/source/API/job or customer operation has occurred.
- Initial format: Explicit UTF-8 JSONL export, at most1MiB/1,000 rows/8KiB per row, containing `timestamp` (aware ISO8601), `origin` (exact current verified project origin), `target` (origin-relative request path), `status` (HTTP100–599) and `user_agent` (bounded text). Other fields are discarded, never copied. Native combined/W3C/CDN formats need separately checked transformations; do not accept them as this declared format. The user declares one <=7-day half-open UTC coverage window ending no later than now and explicitly confirms authorization to use the logs; partial coverage is the only initial claim.
- Privacy: Parse raw input transiently before any application job/artifact/task staging. Never persist raw logs, IPs, cookies, query/fragment values, referrers, full user-agent strings or arbitrary unmatched paths. Only currently authorized existing crawl/indexation inventory URLs may label path groups; all other request paths collapse into one unmatched bucket without retained path bytes. Keep categorical agent labels, HTTP counts and first/last UTC observation times. File replay/overlap cannot silently create total visitor/bot traffic across imports. Scoped derived evidence follows D098 customer retention/export/deletion/35-day backup controls; no separate raw-log retention store.
- Authority: Intake must be owner/admin-controlled, CSRF/size/role/rate/current verified-origin guarded with explicit source authorization and rechecks at staging/worker/delivery. An origin declaration and user-agent string remain customer-supplied observations, not independently verified provider identities. Initial labels cover OAI-SearchBot (search), GPTBot (potential training), ChatGPT-User (user-triggered; not search inclusion) and other/unclassified requests, with method version and `user_agent_only_unverified`. Discard IP rather than claiming DNS/IP verification. Visits do not prove indexing/citations/training membership, absent requests in a partial window do not prove never visited, and request counts are not analytics visits or users.
- Execution: Start with an unregistered offline parser and fixtures, then explicit existing job/private derived evidence and native scoped consumer with owned source references and no raw-data storage. Before exposing intake, synchronize one D077 Security review gate for the newly accepted potentially sensitive log-upload/data-minimization boundary; invoke once only after focused complete consumer checks. No active scan, remote fetch, automated bot identity lookup or real customer log request is authorized.

## D131: Durable Unattended Work-Exhaustion Pause

- Status: Accepted — 2026-10-02, explicit user request extending D124/TOOL-03.

Unreadable/malformed pause or watched inputs skip conservatively.
- Boundaries: Timer remains enabled at ten minutes; no AI call is needed for paused checks. Shared stopping guidance stays provider-neutral, with host commands in `.agents/README.md` and the personal runbook. This does not authorize live/manual tests, paid calls, deployment, Git operations or additional application scope.
